# Migration from v1 → v2

Authoritative mapping table. Every v1 source file lands in exactly one v2 destination. This file is filled in incrementally as each phase progresses; by Phase 11 it is complete and used as the cutover checklist.

**Follow-up (2026-09-04): league lists sort by OPENING DATE, and a reorder UI that is overruled by a sort is a bug (v1)** — A1 (Completed Leagues, `js/render/landingPage.js`) and F1 (admin Leagues list, `js/admin/leagueManager.js`) both order leagues by `IssueDate`, newest first, with a tie broken by league type. Three separate defects came out of that one request. **(1) The type order had no owner.** H1 (Active Leagues cards) declared a private `ACTIVE_TYPE_ORDER = {doubling:0, ubc:1, regular:2}`; the obvious source for a shared version — `Object.keys(CONFIGS)` in `js/compute/leagueTypes.js` — reads doubling, **regular**, ubc, because that is the order the configs happened to be written in, and deriving the display rank from it would have silently swapped UBC and Regular in H1. New exported **`leagueTypeRank()`** states the canonical order **Doubling → UBC → Regular** explicitly, and `sortActiveLeagues()` now reads it instead of restating it, so three lists cannot drift. **(2) F1 was reading `DisplayOrder`** — the PUBLIC landing arrangement — so a newly opened league landed in the admin list wherever the landing page happened to place it. F1 has no drag reorder, so nothing competed with the change; `DisplayOrder` is still what publishing writes and is still passed to the edit/add screens. A row with no date (a publish that never finished, or a draft whose date is unset) sorts to the **top**, since that row is the one asking for work. **(3) The real find: A1's drag reorder was already being thrown away.** Landing edit mode lets an admin drag completed rows, `syncDisplayOrderFromDOM()` writes that into `DisplayOrder`, publish saves it — and `renderCompletedLeagues()` then re-sorted by date on every render, so the arrangement was persisted and silently discarded on the next load. A1 has no click-sortable column, so that sort was the only thing deciding row order; the drag had no effect on A1 at all and nobody had noticed. Resolved with **`landing_settings.completed_custom_order`** (`sql/landing_completed_custom_order.sql`): **false** (default) → the date sort; **true** → `DisplayOrder` verbatim, with ties inside it still falling through to the date sort so the order stays total. A drag on a completed row sets it; a **"Sort completed by date"** button in the edit bar, shown only while it is on, clears it — without that the first drag is a one-way door, since nothing else in the UI can clear the flag. The flag is **explicit, not inferred**: detecting "is this custom?" by comparing `DisplayOrder` against date order was tried on paper and rejected, because this site's `DisplayOrder` was hand-arranged over time and already differs, so inference would have pinned every existing site to custom mode and made the date sort unreachable. **A fourth defect surfaced while adding the column.** Publishing a landing change ends in an **upsert of the whole row**, and FIVE call sites built that payload field-by-field (landing edit mode, add league, delete league, the order-repair path, the rename path) — so the four that only care about the order would have RESET `completed_custom_order` as a side effect of an unrelated league edit. All five now go through **`landingSettingsPayload()`** (`js/admin/landingSettingsPayload.js`), which also absorbs the `ls.DisplayOrder || ls.displayOrder` shape-juggling they each spelled out (the argument may be a loaded settings object or an already-staged payload read back out of the queue). `changeDetails.js` describes the flag in words ("Completed Leagues sorted by date again"). **The reset button was first shipped as a raw unstyled `<button>`** beside two carefully styled siblings, and was corrected against v2's button model: it now declares a VARIANT in the bar's hierarchy — Save primary (filled), Cancel secondary (outlined), this one **ghost** (transparent, borderless, `--color-text-secondary`, em padding, `--fs-080`, `:active` translateY, `:focus-visible` ring) — and sits on the LEFT beside the "Edit Mode" label rather than in the action group, because it UNDOES a mode instead of being a third thing to choose between. Verified across light/dark/vegas/casino/beige. **And the button exposed a latch in Save Changes.** `markDirty()` only ever turned Save ON — so drag a league and press the reset, or drag one and drag it back, and Save stayed lit over a queue with nothing in it. Replaced by **`refreshDirty()`**, which DERIVES the state from `landingEditIsDirty()` — the same predicate `saveEditChanges()` uses, so the button and the save path cannot disagree — and by `withPublishedCompletedOrder()`, which makes the reset restore the completed leagues' PUBLISHED positions instead of stamping the date order into `DisplayOrder`. That second half is what actually lands drag-then-reset back on the published state, and it also stops the reset from destroying the arrangement the mode would show if switched on again (with the mode off, the completed half of `DisplayOrder` is dormant, not dead). Active-league entries are excluded from the restore — an admin who dragged cards in the same session did not ask for that work to be undone. Verified: enter clean (Save off) -> drag (on) -> reset (off, order restored, staged queue empty) -> drag + drag back (on, correctly, because the MODE changed) -> reset (off) -> real drag + Save (staged with `CompletedCustomOrder: true`, Save off again). The bar's `margin-right: auto` also had to move off the label onto `.edit-bar-cancel` as `margin-left: auto`: a margin on a `hidden` element collapses, so leaving it on the label let Cancel/Save slide left whenever the ghost was hidden. Verified live (MCP, local Docker Supabase): default mode puts `June 2026` (Doubling) above `June 2026 Regular` on the same `1 Jun 2026` in both A1 and F1 while the stored `DisplayOrder` has them the other way round; flipping the flag in the DB reproduces `DisplayOrder` exactly, September 2025 first; in edit mode the reset button re-sorts to date order, re-attaches all 11 drag handles and resyncs `DisplayOrder`, and a drag turns the flag back on and stages `CompletedCustomOrder: true`. | ◯ pending — v2 landing + admin not yet built | **v2:** carry `leagueTypeRank()` over as the only league-type display order, and never re-derive it from a config object's key order. The load-bearing rule, though, is the third defect: **a sort and a manual reorder are mutually exclusive claims on the same list, and shipping both without deciding which wins produces a UI that saves the user's work and then discards it.** Whenever a v2 table offers drag ordering, either it has no default sort or it carries an explicit "custom order" flag with a visible way back — and that flag is stored, never inferred from the data, because real data drifts away from the default order on its own. Also carry the payload rule: any staged blob that publishes as a whole-row upsert gets ONE builder, or the next column added will be silently reset by whichever writer predates it.

**Follow-up (2026-09-02): the admin Round Editor is league-type aware — F3 drops PR in a no-PR league, and that is what unblocked Save (v1)** — `js/admin/` never imported `js/compute/leagueTypes.js`, so F3 (Match Results / Round Editor, `js/admin/roundEditor.js`) rendered a **PR** column for every league including **REGULAR**, which tracks no PR at all. The cosmetic half is now gated on the same `getLeagueConfig(params).showPR !== false` that B1 (`js/presets/leagueTablePreset.js`) and the C-series match history (`js/presets/playerMatchHistoryPreset.js`) already use: no PR `<th>`, no PR `<td>`, and no `prA`/`prB` keys in the staged override (omitted entirely, matching the shape `mapDbOverride()` produces for a NULL `pr_a` — the old code wrote a fabricated `parseFloat('') || 0` → PR **0** into leagues that have no PR). The load-bearing half is the second symptom that came in with it: **`validateResultFields()` required a non-empty `prA`/`prB` unconditionally**, so in a REGULAR league — where there is no PR cell to type into — `markPending()` computed `valid === false` on every keystroke, the per-match **Save button never enabled**, and a manual result had no route into Pending Changes at all. The two were one bug: a type-blind editor validating a column its league does not have. Every field read/write in the module now goes through `fieldVal()` / `setField()`, which return `null` / no-op when a column was not rendered, so a type can drop a column without forking a code path — and `null` (column absent) stays deliberately distinct from `''` (present but unfilled), which is exactly what lets one validator serve both league shapes. The TA/TB/TD/NP handler was writing `.value` on the PR inputs directly and would have thrown on click once the column disappeared; it uses `setField()` now. No CSS change was needed: every `.admin-round-table` rule keys off classes, never `nth-child`, so the column count is free to vary. Documented under the F3 exception in `docs/TABLE-DESIGN.md`. | ◯ pending — v2 admin not yet built (read-only until Phase 8) | **v2:** build F3 off the league config from the start, and make the RULE explicit: **admin edit surfaces read the same `getLeagueConfig()` the read-side presets do.** A validator must derive its required-field set from the rendered columns rather than from a hard-coded list, or the no-PR league silently loses the ability to save. Carry the omit-the-key (never `0`) convention into the override writer.

**Follow-up (2026-09-02): a memoised loader hands out SHARED state — `readOverridesForEdit()` (v1)** — found by browser-testing the F3 fix above, which is the only reason it was found at all: with the Save button finally enabling, the save still did not reach Pending Changes. `loadOverrides()` (`js/data/supabaseLoader.js`) is wrapped in `memoised()`, which caches the **promise** — so every caller awaits the very same array instance. Three call sites read it and then edited it **in place**: `stageOverride()` and `stageBulkTechLoss()` (`js/admin/roundEditor.js`) and `mergeStagedOverrides()` (`js/admin/excelImporter.js`), all doing `overrides[idx] = o` / `overrides.push(o)` on the array the read cache is still holding. Two silent failures followed. (1) Unpublished admin edits were written straight into the cache the **public** page renders from. (2) The edit then became invisible: the caller passed that array to `stageManualOverrides()`, whose baseline comes from the same `loadOverrides()` — i.e. **the same, already-mutated array**. Diffing it against itself gave `deltaCount === 0`, so the "no net change vs published" branch **deleted the staged change**. The admin saw `Saved: A vs B`, the row turned green, the badge stayed **0**, and Pending Changes stayed empty. Measured live: the DB held 27 overrides for the league while the in-page cache held 28, the extra one being the unsaved edit. Fixed canonically rather than at each site (three copies of one read is what let this hide): **`readOverridesForEdit(leagueId)`** in `js/admin/stagingStore.js` is now the single way to read overrides in order to change them — staged content if pending (a fresh array from `JSON.parse` anyway), otherwise the published set **shallow-copied per override** (`.map(o => ({...o}))`; an override is a flat record). `stageManualOverrides()` additionally copies its own baseline, so both ends of the path are private and no future caller can re-alias them. All three sites now call it, and `roundEditor.js` no longer imports `loadOverrides` at all. | ◯ pending — v2 admin not yet built | **v2:** decide the ownership rule at the loader, not at the call site. Either the cache hands out frozen/immutable reads (so an in-place edit throws loudly instead of corrupting quietly) or every editing path goes through an explicit "give me a copy I own" accessor like this one. A cached read path and an in-place editor in the same codebase is the trap; nothing about either half looks wrong on its own line, and the failure surfaces as *silence* — a success message with no saved change. Note also the general lesson for testing: the first browser run "passed" against a **stale ES module** because `page.goto()` to a URL differing only in the `#hash` does not reload the document. Force a real reload before believing an admin-side result.

**Follow-up (2026-09-02): a medal can carry more than one prize row (v1)** — a league has exactly three medals but can hand out more than three KINDS of prize (gold ₪2,200; the two runners-up ₪1,100 each; four more at ₪180). B1 (Prizes & Medals, `js/render/dashboardPage.js` → `renderPrizes`) and F6 (Medals & Prizes, `js/admin/leagueManager.js` → `ffMedalsTableHTML`) were both hard-wired to exactly three rows. New shared module **`js/compute/prizeRows.js`** owns the whole concept: `MEDAL_TIERS`, `getExtraPrizeRows()` / `withExtraPrizeRows()` (read/write), `countExtraPrizeRows()`, `buildPrizeRows(params)` (the interleaved base+extra row list), `formatPrize()`. Each tier carries an optional list of EXTRA rows, each with its own Places count and prize amount, rendered directly under that medal's own row in the same four columns. **Storage adds no column and no migration**: the rows live inside the existing `leagues.prizes` jsonb under an `Extra` key (`{"Gold":2200,…,"Extra":{"Gold":[{"count":2,"prize":1100}]}}`), and the key is omitted entirely when no tier has one, so a league that never uses the feature keeps byte-identical JSON. **An extra row awards real medals**, so every ranking surface had to agree with B1: `getMedalPlaces(params, defaults)` in the same module is now the ONE answer to "how many places does this tier award" (own count + the Places of its extra rows; a tier whose own count is 0 awards 0 however many extra rows sit under it, matching how `buildPrizeRows` hides it). Every former reader of the raw `*Count` params was converted to it — D (`js/presets/leagueTablePreset.js`), B2 (`js/render/dashboardPage.js`, which both tints AND sizes itself off it), C1/C6 rank cells (`js/presets/playerLeaguesPreset.js`), the cross-league medal tallies (`js/compute/crossLeague.js` ×2, `js/compute/allTimeRankings.js`), the landing page's four league-record collectors, and the table-lab mirrors. Each call site passes its OWN fallback (bronze has historically defaulted to 1, 3 and 4 in different tables) rather than having one imposed on it. The annual leaderboard's fixed 1/1/1 podium is a different concept and stays as it is. Admin F6 grew a fourth, button-width column (`+` on each medal's own row, `×` on each extra row), wired by `wireMedalsTable()` which dispatches a bubbling `change` so the dormant-until-edited "Save Settings" tracker arms on a row add/remove (a click fires neither `input` nor `change`); `readExtraPrizeRows()` reads them back by position and `applyExtraPrizeRows()` rebuilds them for the Add-League preset picker, so cloning a league clones its prize rows. `js/admin/render/changeDetails.js` gained `describePrizes()` so a pending diff reads "extra prize rows 1 → 3" instead of an opaque "changed Extra". `table-lab/lab-loader.js` `buildB1` now uses the shared builder and tints by ROW count per tier, not one row per tier. | ◯ pending — v2 league dashboard + admin not yet built | **v2:** port `prizeRows.js` as-is and build BOTH tables off `buildPrizeRows()` from day one — the three-hard-coded-rows shape is what had to be undone here. Keep `Extra` inside the `prizes` jsonb (no new column) and keep it omitted when empty. Above all, never read `gold_count`/`silver_count`/`bronze_count` directly for medal assignment: go through `getMedalPlaces()`, or a tier will mean one thing in the prizes table and another in the standings.

**Follow-up (2026-08-29): a flag belongs to a player-in-a-league, not to a player (v1)** — `leagues.custom_flags` is the ONLY flag store on the site (`players_metadata` has no flag column), so a player who changes country has one entry per league and nothing that says which is current. Every cross-league view answered that with a flat `Object.assign` merge of every league's `CustomFlags`, which was wrong three ways: the winner was whichever league came LAST in DisplayOrder — the OLDEST — so "in general" showed a player's FIRST flag; a per-MATCH row got that same single answer regardless of which league the match belonged to, so a flag change rewrote history backwards; and a merge cannot express a player who reverted to the default, because IL is an ABSENCE from `CustomFlags`, so an older custom flag outlived it forever. New `js/utils/playerFlags.js` → `buildPlayerFlagIndex(leaguesNewestFirst)` splits the one question into two: **`.latest(name)`** (context-free — the flag they last played under, resolved through the newest league they appear in, so it CAN return IL again) and **`.inLeague(name, leagueId)`** (context-bound — that league's flag, full stop). Recency = position in DisplayOrder, the same rule `resolveDefaultFlags()` in `js/admin/leagueManager.js` already used on the WRITE path; the read path now agrees with it. Context-bound call sites fixed: `player.html` C2 match history, C3 head-to-head winner cell, C5 per-match records (presets `playerAllMatchesPreset.js` / `matchupPreset.js` now pass `row._leagueId` into their enrich callbacks — passing the bare name is what forced the merge). Context-free call sites fixed: the card header, C4 All Opponents, all-time rank tables, the H2H opponent picker and the PR-Results picker, `js/compute/allTimeRankings.js` (which also merged only within one league TYPE), the landing page's annual leaderboard, the smart search's `_playerFlags` in `js/render/navigation.js` (which also feeds the sidebar, the mobile sheet, `analyticsPage.js`'s entity chips and the league editor's Add-a-player field), and the admin players registry search in `js/admin/playerManager.js`. An audit of all 11 player searches classified each as general or league-scoped and confirmed the resolver matches: general = global smart search, sidebar, H2H picker, PR-Results picker, analytics entities, admin registry, league editor Add-a-player; league-scoped = the three dashboard pickers (What-If A/B, B6c, snapshot) and the round editor's filter, all of which read their own league's `params.CustomFlags` and were already right. Already correct and untouched: single-league surfaces (league table, `player_league.html`, dashboard, round editor, mail sync) — they never had a merge to get wrong. Verified live (MCP, local Supabase): Moriarty (TZ through Apr 2026 → GE in May/Jun → no entry in Jul) reads TZ / GE / IL per league row in C2, C3 and C5, and IL everywhere context-free; Izhako (ES only in Jul 2026) reads ES in the July row and in search, IL in older rows. | ◯ pending | **v2:** port `playerFlags.js` as-is and treat it as the only flag resolver. The rule to keep: any table row that names a LEAGUE or a MATCH resolves the flag through that league; anything that names only a PERSON resolves `.latest()`. Never merge `CustomFlags` across leagues — a merge cannot represent a reverted flag, and it silently answers the wrong one of the two questions.

**Follow-up (2026-08-20): a league says how long it runs — `duration_mode` / `duration_days` (v1)** — the League Progress card measured every league against the calendar month its `IssueDate` falls in, an assumption that lived nowhere but that card. A league running two weeks, or running open-endedly, was scored against a month it never had. Leagues now carry a duration: **`month`** (the CALENDAR MONTH the issue date falls in — the league runs from its issue date to the LAST DAY of that month, so it is month-*long* only when it opens on the 1st: issued 21 Feb 2026 it runs 8 days, issued 31 Aug it runs one day), **`days`** (`duration_days` counted inclusively from the start), or **`unlimited`** (no window at all). New DB columns via `sql/league_duration.sql` — `duration_mode text not null default 'month'` plus a CHECK that pairs `duration_days` with `days` mode alone, so a stale count can never linger behind a mode that doesn't use it. The default makes the migration a no-op for every existing league: this project's leagues all start on the 1st, so their windows are byte-identical to the old behaviour. `get_site_bundle()` needed no change (it ships rows through `to_jsonb(l)`). The single client-side definition is **`js/compute/leagueDuration.js`** — `leagueDateWindow()` returns `{start, end}` inclusive at local midnight, or **null** for "no window", which the dashboard must treat as "show no time reading", never as zero; `dashboardPage.js` dropped its private copy of that logic and imports it, so the bar a visitor sees and the setting an admin picks cannot drift. Both admin forms (Add + Edit) get a Duration select plus a Days field that appears only in `days` mode, and a live hint naming the resulting window AND its length (`Runs 21 Feb 2026 → 28 Feb 2026 (8 days).`) so the admin sees the real window before saving — in calendar-month mode the length depends on where in the month the league opens, which the dates alone make easy to miss. Pending and Historical both describe it in words (`Duration: calendar month → 45 days`) via `changeDetails.js`. **v2 destination:** the duration model belongs in `src/compute/leagueDuration.js` as pure functions over params, imported by both the progress card and the admin form — never re-derived in a renderer. Two rules to carry over: a null window is a distinct state from a zero-length one (unlimited leagues show the games bar and an explicit "No time limit", and are never flagged "behind the calendar" — there is no calendar to be behind), and "month" is a CALENDAR month, not a 30-ish-day span — it is `new Date(y, m + 1, 0)` (day 0 of the next month = the last day of this one), never date arithmetic from the start day.

**Follow-up (2026-08-20): A5 Luckiest Matches shows both sides' PR (v1)** — the Luckiest Matches card (`js/render/landingPage.js → renderMatchRecordsTables`) gained two columns, **Player PR** and **Opp PR**, between `Result` and `League`, so the luck gap can be read against the play quality that produced it. The data comes from `js/compute/matchRecords.js → collectLuckMatches`, which now emits `prSelf`/`prOpp` **oriented the same way as `scoreSelf`/`scoreOpp`** — the row's "player" is the side that benefited from the luck (`aBenefits`), so the PR must be flipped with it; reading `m.prA` straight would mislabel half the rows. A missing PR renders `—` (not `N/A`, which in this project means "this match has no number"). `matchRecordRow` took an optional `extraCells` argument rather than growing a second row builder, so the sibling **Best PR Matches** card is byte-identical to before. No CSS change was needed: `.match-records-table`'s sticky columns are 1–3 (`# | Player | Luck Gap`), and the insert is at 7–8. The lab's A5 preset (`table-lab/lab-loader.js → buildA5SF`) mirrors the **Best PR** card only and is unaffected. **v2 destination:** `A5_matchRecords.js` (SF) — build the Luckiest variant with the two PR columns from the start, and keep the orientation flip inside the collector, not the renderer: any per-match row that picks a "player" side must carry EVERY per-side field through the same flip, or the columns silently disagree with each other.

**Follow-up (2026-08-16): an identity field's text and its chrome are ONE state — `setValue()` (v1)** — clicking an opponent in the All Opponents table on `player.html` put that player's name into the H2H field but left the PREVIOUS opponent's flag and title badges beside it (`YossiEliezer23` wearing boutsky's `G2`). The handler in `js/render/playerGeneralPage.js` did `input.value = name; combo.close();` — a raw text write, so nothing repainted the adornments. The dropdown and mobile-sheet paths were never affected: `choose()` calls `setIdentity(value)` before `onPick`. Only a jump from OUTSIDE the list could desync them, and the API made that easy to get wrong — the text and the chrome were two separate calls, so doing one was silently valid. `js/utils/combobox.js` now returns **`setValue(key)`**, which writes `labelFor(key)` AND repaints the identity AND closes the list in one call; it deliberately fires neither `onPick` nor `onChange`, since the caller is already handling whatever its own click means. The same handler had a second, related defect it inherited: it wrote the raw username key while the dropdown writes the display name, so under "Show name as → full name" an external jump disagreed with what the clicked row said; `setValue` going through `labelFor` fixes both at once. `selectOpponent` was converted to `setValue` too, so no call site outside the module writes `.value` on an identity field any more. Audited every `.value =` in the four files that mount identity fields (`dashboardPage.js`, `playerGeneralPage.js`, `leagueManager.js`, `playerManager.js`): all remaining writes are either inside an `onPick` (already painted by `choose()`) or paired with an explicit `setIdentity()` — this was the only defect. Verified live (MCP, desktop): list-pick Hummus (NC+M2, BE) → table-click boutsky → field reads `boutsky` with `G2` and the IL flag → table-click Hummus → back to NC+M2 and BE, with no stale badge in any direction. **The rule is enforced at runtime, not merely documented.** A header note cannot stop the next call site from writing `input.value`, and this class had already shipped once, so an `identity` field now intercepts writes to its own `.value` (an instance-level accessor over `HTMLInputElement.prototype`'s, so reads and every other input behaviour are untouched). Writes the module makes itself go through one `writeText()` helper that flags them; any OTHER write whose text does not match the current subject's label drops the flag and badges, exactly as typing into the field does. It deliberately does not try to REPAIR the chrome — the text is a display label and the identity is keyed by username, and there is no reliable way back (two players can share a full name, and a caller may write anything) — so the guarantee is narrower and worth more: the chrome can be **missing**, never **wrong**. A missing flag is a caller that should have used `setValue`; a wrong flag is a lie about who the field is showing. No static gate: linking an `input` variable to the `identity: true` mount it was passed to is not reliably detectable by a regex checker, and a false-clean gate is worse than none. **v2 destination:** whatever combobox the rebuild ships, expose ONE method that sets the displayed text and the identity chrome together and keep the raw text setter off the public surface. Anywhere a picker's value can be set from outside its own list — a table row, a deep link, a "jump to" control — the two halves WILL drift apart if the API lets a caller move only one of them, so back the convention with a mechanism that degrades to "no decoration" rather than to "someone else's decoration".

**Follow-up (2026-08-16): a search field's identity adornments were sized against the page, not against the field (v1)** — `.title-abbr` is `font-size: 1em` and `.search-flag` is `height: 1.05em` precisely so a badge always matches the text it belongs to, and in a table cell that works perfectly: the badge is a CHILD of the `<td>`, so the em resolves against the cell (measured **12.75px name / 12.75px badge — 1.00×**). Inside a search field it cannot be a child — an `<input>` holds text and nothing else — so `identity: true` renders the flag and badges as absolutely-positioned **siblings** in `.app-combo-wrap`, and their em silently resolved against the WRAP's inherited page size instead of the field's own `var(--fs-090)`. Same class, same rule, different neighbour: **15px badge beside a 13.5px name (1.11×) at 1440px**, and because `--fs-*` are `clamp()` tokens that shrink faster than the root, the gap WIDENS as the viewport narrows — **13.17px beside 10.04px (1.31×) at 390px**. It compounds, because `applyIdentityLayout` grows the input's padding to the slot's measured width: a player with two badges (NC + M2) on a 390px phone took **padding-left 32 + padding-right 87 out of a 158.7px field — 75% — leaving the name 37.7px** and clipping it. Fixed in `js/utils/combobox.js`: `applyIdentityLayout()` copies `getComputedStyle(input).fontSize` onto both slots before it measures them, read fresh every pass rather than cached alongside the base padding — the token is viewport-dependent and a field may carry a deliberate skin (the pinned mobile bar forces 16px), so copying the computed value keeps every case in step with no second rule. **Two follow-on defects surfaced from the same root and are fixed with it.** (a) **The gap between the name and the first badge had no owner.** It was a JS constant PLUS `.title-abbr`'s own `margin-left: 0.5em` — a rule authored for a badge sitting INLINE after a name in a table cell, where no positioning code exists to do the job. Two contributors in two files meant the distance measured differently depending on a browser's stylesheet state (12.75px here, ~6px in the reporter's window) and it moved on its own when the font fix above landed. `layout.css` now zeroes that margin inside the slot and `BADGE_GAP_EM` is the single owner. It is **em-relative, never px**: a gap after a word is typography and must stay a constant number of characters, and a fixed 12px was 0.89em at 1440px but 1.20em at 390px — the same distance reading as a wider and wider space as the type shrank. 0.75em rather than the table's 0.5em because the field's badge is a filled pill centred on the field (its box overshoots the name's ink) while the table's is baseline-aligned after the text, so the same distance reads tighter. (b) **A `ResizeObserver` on the field is the wrong trigger for a viewport change and missed it completely.** `.app-search-input` is a fixed 300px, so narrowing the window from 1277 → 500 took the clamp() font from 13.5px → 11.05px **without moving any box**: the observer never fired, and the adornments kept the size and offset computed for the old width — 13.5px badges beside 11.05px text, parked 1.8em past a name that had shrunk (resize the other way and they sit ON the name instead, which is what the original report was). This is why it was irreproducible from a fresh load at any width: only resizing WITHOUT reloading triggers it. New module-level `onViewportResize()` registers every mounted identity field against one `window.resize` listener, coalesced into an animation frame so a drag-resize does not run the layout per pixel, self-pruning entries whose wrap has left the document (chart panels are removable). The `ResizeObserver` stays for what it was written for (0 → visible). No feedback loop from either, since the callback only writes padding and the input is `box-sizing: border-box`. Verified live (MCP, 1440 + 390) on four independent identity fields — H2H opponent, league What-If, league chart picker, admin Players — all now report slot font-size === input font-size; on 390px the two badges dropped 13.17 → 10.04px, the slot 69.3 → 53.3px, and the name's room went **37.7 → 56.7px**. The table was untouched and still measures 1.00×; the mobile search SHEET was investigated and is correct as-is (its badges are children of `.search-sheet-option-label`, 1.00×, and its 26px avatar is 0.78 of the two-line content box — the standard list-row proportion). A local-only fixture, `sql/test_fixture_two_titles.sql`, gives `Hummus` a national championship so a two-badge player exists to test with; no player in the real data carries two. The three rules are now written into `combobox.js`'s module header as canon (§ "The identity chrome, and why it has three rules"), so they bind every current and future `identity: true` field rather than living in one call site. Verified live (MCP, desktop window, resizing WITHOUT reload across 1277 / 900 / 640 / 500 / 430 / 1440) on all four identity fields — H2H opponent, league What-If, chart player picker, admin Players: slot font === input font, badge margin 0, gap 0.75em, at every width and in both directions. **v2 destination:** whatever combobox the rebuild ships, any adornment layered OVER an input must (1) be handed the input's own computed font-size — an em on a sibling overlay is measuring the wrong element, and a fluid type scale turns that into a viewport-dependent defect that looks like nothing at desktop width; (2) get its spacing from exactly one owner, expressed in em, never split between JS and a shared CSS rule; and (3) be re-laid-out on `window.resize`, not on a `ResizeObserver` over a fixed-width field, because a clamp() font changes with no box changing. More generally: a mark that belongs to a line of text must inherit that text's size, which is only automatic while it is a descendant; every overlay has to restate it explicitly, and re-state it again whenever the viewport moves.

**Follow-up (2026-08-14): A7 drops its Title column — titles stack inside the Player cell (v1)** — the Players directory (`js/render/landingPage.js` → `buildPlayerCols`, CSS in `css/index-dashboard.css`) went from four columns to **three: Player / Status / Last Active**. The `Title` column measured **182px of the table's 395px — 46% of the width — on a 430px phone, while reading `—` on 53 of its 56 rows**, and was the sole cause of A7's horizontal overflow. Two workarounds had accumulated on top of it and are both now deleted: A7's font shrank to `--fs-078` on mobile (making it the only A table not on `font-small` at every width) and `.lp-realname` was `display:none` below 600px, which removed the point of a directory people look each other up in. The title description now renders as extra lines inside the Player cell — **one line per title**, championships first then BMAB, each an `<em class="lp-titledesc">` indented `1.3em` (the flag is `1em` wide + `0.3em` margin, so the lines start exactly under the name link — measured 17.68px on both), `font-weight: 400` (the cell is `td.player-cell`, which is 600; the old Title column was an ordinary cell), and `white-space: normal` overriding the cell's `nowrap` so the longest title (`1990 Monte Carlo Doubles World Champion`) cannot dictate the column width for all rows. There is no `Title` header — the description reads as itself. Supporting change in `js/data/titleConstants.js`: new **`getTitleDescriptionParts(meta)`** returns the titles as an array (championships first, BMAB last) and `getFullTitleDescription()` is now `.join(', ')` over it — splitting the joined string back apart would break on any title containing a comma. Also registered A7 in the typo editor (`js/render/typoEditor.js`, tier t6) and corrected A1/A2 there from t5 → t6: both render `.font-small` (0.85rem) in production, so the editor's t5 (0.93rem) default would have silently enlarged them. **v2 destination:** `A7_playersDirectory.js` preset — build it with three columns and the stacked Player cell; do not port the Title column, the mobile font carve-out, or the hidden real-name. Full rationale + measurements in `docs/TABLE-DESIGN.md` § A7 and the A7 note in `docs/plans/table-lab-unification.md` Phase 5.

**Follow-up (2026-08-14): every player search runs on ONE generator, and the identity travels with the pick (v1)** — an audit of all 11 player searches (excluding the sidebar global search) found them agreeing on nothing. Three were bare `<select>` elements, which cannot hold a flag or a title badge at all; the admin Players search was a hand-rolled `<input>` + `<ul>` with no keyboard nav and — because it lacked the `.app-search-input` hook — **no mobile search sheet**, the one player search you had to type into a 14px field on a phone. The field BOX was restated in seven places (7px/12px + 1.5px border here, `--space-xs`/`--space-sm` there, `--space-sm`/`--space-md` in admin), and the two chart pickers matched a rule covering `select` and `button` but not `input`, so on conversion they fell back to the browser's 2px inset default. Flags came from three different sources (per-league `CustomFlags`, the global nav index, and an `Object.assign` merge across all leagues whose winner depended on iteration order) and titles from three (`titleHtmlFor`'s cache, `getTitleAbbreviationsHtml` direct, and a per-mount async map with its own race). Consolidated into `js/utils/combobox.js` + `.app-search-input` in `layout.css`, with the permitted variations declared in the module header (what a pick does; browse-all vs type-to-search; popup vs inplace; sublabel / badge / luck extras) and everything else — size, spacing, flag sizing, badge markup, the mobile sheet — owned centrally. Four behaviours were added there rather than per site: `identity: true` (the picked player's flag and title badges stay INSIDE the field as adornments, with the input's padding grown to their measured width so text never runs under them, and the badges trailing the *measured* text so the gap after the name equals the gap before it); `browseOnOpen: true` (entering a filled field empties it so the list browses everything, blur restores it — bound to **both `focus` and `click`**, because the pick path deliberately keeps focus and a second click fires no focus event, and threaded into the mobile sheet, which otherwise seeded itself from the field's value and opened a one-row list); `playerIdentityHtml()` (the flag · name · titles chip for echoing a pick OUTSIDE a field — What-If's staged cards, the remaining-matches heading, the H2H table's Winner cell — via `enrich.playerIdentity` so the table preset stays data-agnostic); and lazy base-padding measurement, since `getComputedStyle` on a **detached** node reports 0 and one call site mounts its picker before appending its panel, parking that flag flush against the border while every other field sat at 12px. Also fixed project-wide: pickers wrote the raw username back to a field whose list had shown the full name (`labelFor` was applied to the list only), so callers now keep the username key separate from the displayed label — What-If's `picked{a,b}`, the chart panel's `entry.player`, B6c's `resolvePlayer` — and every list sorts by `displayPlayerName(...).localeCompare(...)` rather than by a raw key the user cannot see. What-If's A/B pair became one config mounted twice, so filling *either* side narrows and tags the other. **When v2 builds any player picker: mount the one shared search component from the start and let call sites pass only declared deltas — never a second implementation, and never a `<select>` for a player. Carry the identity through the whole flow (list → field → whatever the pick produces), keep the display label separate from the key, sort by the label, and measure any layout the component depends on lazily and only while connected. The variation list belongs in the component's own header, so the next call site adds a flag rather than a fork.**

**Follow-up (2026-08-02): player-header title chips are click-tracked + tap-to-info in both header variants (v1)** — the WC/NC/G2… title chips in the player headers (`renderV7Header` per-league page, `renderV12Header` cross-league page, both in `js/render/playerHeader.js`) already opened their full tooltip on tap via `data-tooltip-tap` (phones have no hover); confirmed both work. Added `data-track="Title: <abbr>"` to both chips so an actual CLICK on the icon is logged on every device (desktop still shows the tooltip on hover with no click — tracking only fires on a real click). The abbreviation is a stable, translation-proof slug: `buildHeaderTitles` now carries `abbreviation` (WC/NC from `getPrimaryChampionshipInfo`, the BMAB code from `getBmabInfo`). In the analytics log the row renders the **real badge**, not an emoji: new `titleAbbrBadgeHtml(abbr)` + `getTierForAbbr(abbr)` in `js/data/titleConstants.js` map a bare abbreviation to its tier and emit the same `.title-abbr title-abbr-<tier>` pill worn next to player names (WC/NC also get `title-abbr-champ`); `analyticsPage.js`'s `clickTargetHtml` renders `Title: <badge>` and `CLICK_TYPE_ICONS` gives the row a 🎖️ type-marker. Scope was deliberately limited to the two player headers — the `.title-abbr` pills in tables/search/pickers were NOT given tap/tracking. **When v2 builds player headers + analytics: put the title-chip click hook and the abbreviation slug in from the start, and render the actual tier-coloured badge in any log/audit surface rather than a stand-in glyph.**

**Follow-up (2026-08-02): analytics click tracking moved to capture phase + two families that were never tracked (v1)** — the document-level click listener in `js/analytics.js` ran in the BUBBLE phase, so any `stopPropagation()` between the target and `document` silently hid that click from analytics. The Leaderboard "Export Image" button was the reported symptom: it carries `.img-export-btn` (a tracked selector) but its export-group wrapper calls `stopPropagation()` so the collapsible doesn't toggle — killing tracking. Fixed by registering the listener in the **capture phase** (`addEventListener('click', handler, true)`): it now runs before any element's own handler, immune to `stopPropagation` everywhere at once (sidebar theme swatches, search overlay, comboboxes, the export group). Tracking only reads the event — never `preventDefault`s or stops it — so running first is side-effect-free. Separately, two interaction families matched NO selector and so were never tracked even without a stopPropagation: the "Show all (N)" table-expanders (`.show-more-btn`, landing leaderboards + player page) and the EN/HE language toggle in "?" popups (`.popup-lang-flag`). Added as their own branches → `Expand: show all` and `Language: <en|he>` (dataset.lang is the translation-proof slug), with icons `↕️`/`🌐` in `analyticsPage.js`'s `CLICK_TYPE_ICONS`. All three verified end-to-end against the local analytics_events table. **When v2 builds analytics, register the click listener in the capture phase from the start, and give any element that stops propagation an explicit tracked hook rather than relying on the bubble reaching document.**

**Follow-up (2026-08-01): league id is the natural key — smooth rename + name-uniqueness, cosmetic title retired (v1)** — the league's identity is now its `id` alone (folder id / `?league=` param / display name everywhere); the separate cosmetic `title`/`LeagueTitle` is being removed. Two mechanisms landed. (1) **Smooth rename** — `sql/league_rename.sql`: a dynamic `do` block gives **every** FK that targets `public.leagues` `ON UPDATE CASCADE` (not just the core four — the external-source sync tables `external_source_sync_events` / `external_source_sync_log` / `sync_plan_members` reference leagues too; a hardcoded list would silently break on the next such table), plus a `security definer` `rename_league(old_id, new_id)` RPC that: validates non-empty, enforces **case-insensitive uniqueness** (a URL can't distinguish "July"/"july"), renames the PK (cascade carries matches/overrides/history/snapshots/sync rows), rewrites the two non-FK free-text columns `analytics_events.league_id` + `from_league_id` (a renamed league is ONE entity, so its analytics stay unified — deliberately unlike immutable session/region), and — critically — sets `app.suppress_audit` for the transaction so the hundreds of cascade row-updates don't flood `audit_log` (matches/overrides/history all carry `trg_*_audit`), logging instead **exactly one** clean `leagues UPDATE {id:old}→{id:new}` row. It also sets `title = new_id` to hold the `title = id` invariant until the column is dropped. (2) **Admin wiring** — the Edit-League "League Name" field (`js/admin/leagueManager.js`) no longer writes a cosmetic `LeagueTitle`; a changed value stages a **real rename**: client-side uniqueness pre-check (against DisplayOrder-derived ids), a guard blocking rename while the league has other pending changes (avoids undefined publish order against the old id), a confirm, then three grouped staged changes — the rename (`leagues/<old>/__rename__`, new `kind:'league_rename'` in `stagingStore.parseChangePath`/`publishAll`, staged FIRST so the params upsert lands on the renamed row), a landing-order swap (DisplayOrder entry → raw new id; the dash↔space map treats a raw-id entry as itself), and the settings under the new id. New `supabaseAdmin.renameLeague()` calls the RPC; new `league-rename` category (🏷️ Renamed, rank 100) in `changeVocabulary.js` so Pending + Historical label it correctly (the batch headline comes from the client `deriveGroupIntent`, so no `audit_row_intent` SQL change was needed). Create-league also gained the same uniqueness guard, and `LeagueTitle` is dropped from new `league_params.json` (`mapParamsToLeagueRow` already falls `title` back to the id). **Bulk rename + column drop (same day):** `sql/rename_leagues_to_month_year.sql` renamed every league to the final scheme — DOUBLING → `<Month Year>` (dropped the "Shabi Israel " prefix), REGULAR → `<Month Year> Regular` (renamed FIRST inside the txn to free the bare name for the doubling league of the same month, else the uniqueness guard collides) — and stripped the same prefix from `landing_settings.display_order` (guarded so a re-run logs no ghost audit row). Then `sql/drop_league_title.sql` **retired the `leagues.title` column entirely**: it re-creates `rename_league` in a title-free form (the cloud copy still did `set …, title = new_id`, which a bare DROP would leave raising "column title does not exist" on every future rename) and `drop column if exists title`. `get_site_bundle` needed no change — it builds leagues via `to_jsonb(l)` (dynamic), so the column just stops appearing and the mappers' `LeagueTitle: row.title` become undefined → fall back to the id. `js/admin/supabaseAdmin.js → mapParamsToLeagueRow` stopped emitting `title`. **Safe cutover order (no breakage window):** (1) `alter table leagues alter column title drop not null` → (2) deploy the title-free JS → (3) run `drop_league_title.sql`. The id is now the sole identity: display + primary key + `?league=` URL. Sync still keys on `source_league_name`, untouched. **When v2 builds its admin/data phase: model league identity as the id only from the start, port `rename_league` + the cascade with the audit-suppression baked in, never store or read a separate `title`, and build league rows via dynamic `to_jsonb` so schema changes don't require touching the bundle RPC.**

**Follow-up (2026-07-31): the cross-league Matches tab handles mixed league types + undated legacy matches (v1)** — the player page's Matches tab (`js/render/playerGeneralPage.js → renderMatchHistory`, table **C2** via `js/presets/playerAllMatchesPreset.js`, chart via `js/render/playerBarChart.js`) pools every league a player appeared in, so REGULAR rows sit next to doubling/UBC ones — and REGULAR leagues record no PR and no Luck at all. Three fixes, none of which drop a column or a match: (1) **Table** — PR / Opp PR / Luck read `—` for REGULAR rows via a new shared `rateCell()`, kept deliberately distinct from the existing `N/A` (which means "this particular match has no number": a technical win or a data gap). The columns themselves stay — unlike A2/annual, where the whole group is one type and the column is omitted. (2) **Chart** — REGULAR matches now KEEP their slot (dropping them silently renumbered the X axis), but `metricValue()` returns null for them so no bar is drawn, and the running mean is built from `maPoints[{slot, value}]` over rated slots only — stepping over unrated ones instead of renumbering, and treating them as 0 (the old `?? 0`) would have dragged the average toward a value nobody played. With zero rated matches in view there is no MA line at all and the host shows a note instead of an empty grid. (3) **Dates** — leagues that ran before the database was connected have no `match_history` rows, so every match in them was dateless (`—`), fell out of the year filter, and sank to the bottom of the table. `flattenAllMatches` (`js/compute/crossLeague.js`) now derives `matchDate = m.updatedAt ?? league.params.IssueDate` plus a `_dateApprox` flag (title-tooltip on the cell), and sorts/filters/charts on it; the same fallback already existed in `js/compute/matchRecords.js`. The data side is backfilled by `sql/backfill_match_history_dates.sql` — inserts a `match_history` row stamped at `leagues.issue_date` for every played pairing that has none, mirroring `matchHistoryReconcile.js`'s two passes exactly (so a later sync sees them as unchanged and does not redate them) and logging every inserted id to `match_history_backfill_log` for an exact undo. **v2: `v2/src/utils/formatting.js` + whatever builds v2's match rows must carry the same `updatedAt ?? IssueDate` fallback, and v2's C2/chart equivalents must be type-aware from the start — a cross-league view cannot assume every row carries PR.**

**Follow-up (2026-07-30): a player never shows more than ONE championship icon (v1)** — the display rule is: at most **2** title icons next to a name — one BMAB rank + one championship. BMAB was already inherently single (`bmabTitle` is a scalar field), but all three championship-badge surfaces looped over `meta.championshipTitles` and emitted one badge per title, so a player with three national titles rendered `NC NC NC`, and one with both a national and a world title rendered two icons instead of just `WC`. New helper `getPrimaryChampionshipInfo(meta)` in `js/data/titleConstants.js` collapses the array to a single badge — **World outranks National** — and builds a tooltip listing *every* championship (World ones first, joined by ` · `) so nothing is lost on hover. The three call sites now go through it: `getTitleAbbreviationsHtml` (compact `WC`/`NC` pills in every table, search dropdown, and picker), `getTitleBadgesHtml` (full-size badges on player-card headers), and `buildHeaderTitles` in `js/render/playerHeader.js` (V7 header chips — this one had been calling `getChampionshipInfo` per title directly and is why the fix isn't confined to `titleConstants.js`). `getFullTitleDescription` deliberately still lists every title — the Notable Figures list wants the full prose. Latent-only in production data today (one player, one title), but it triggers the moment a second title is entered. **v2 has no HTML generators for this — `v2/src/components/PlayerCell/playerCell.js` renders whatever `titles[]` array it is handed. When v2 wires real metadata into PlayerCell/PlayerHero, port `getPrimaryChampionshipInfo` into `v2/src/data/titleConstants.js` and collapse at the array-building step, so no consumer can ever hand PlayerCell two championship entries.**

**Follow-up (2026-07-23): REGULAR leagues drop all PR surfaces (v1)** — REGULAR-type leagues record no PR (`js/compute/leagueTypes.js` → `REGULAR_CONFIG.showPR === false`), so three v1 surfaces that unconditionally exposed PR were gated on `showPR`: (1) **A2 Annual Leaderboards** (`js/presets/annualLeaderboardPreset.js` + its landing call site + the lab reference `table-lab/lab-loader.js → buildA2`) — the `meanPR` column is omitted for REGULAR groups (was rendering an empty "N/A" column); (2) the dashboard **Charts tab** Player-match-history metric picker (`js/render/dashboardPage.js → renderPlayerSection`) — the "PR" `<option>` is dropped and the picker defaults to "Luck"; (3) the two dashboard **PR ↔ Result Correlation** sections (Player + League, `insightsPanel` gated on `showPR`, `renderPrCorrelationSection` early-returns) are removed entirely for REGULAR. **When v2 builds A2 and the league dashboard Charts phase, gate every PR column/metric/correlation section on the league config's `showPR` from the start — do not render PR chrome for REGULAR and back it out later.**

**Follow-up (2026-07-20): standalone WC26 (מונדיאל 2026) betting dashboard added (v1)** — a new self-contained page under `WC26/` (`wc26.html` + `wc26.js` + `wc26-data.js` + `wc26.css`), reachable only by direct URL (`/WC26/wc26.html`) — no INDEX/nav link, exactly like `analytics.html`. It is **RTL Hebrew** (`<html lang="he" dir="rtl">`) and reuses the shared design system via `../` relative paths (`variables/typography-tokens/layout/components/theme/themes/theme-picker/sections.css`, `table-lab/formats/mf/mf.css`, and `js/render/themePicker.js`) so all picker themes apply unchanged. Three `.app-section--card` sections: (1) overall ranking — every column sortable, default total DESC; (2) result accuracy (בול/כיוון/טעות) — sortable, default בול DESC → כיוון DESC tiebreak; (3) pre-match matrix — **not sortable**, fixed total-DESC order, two-row header (category label + actual result), empty cells render `–` not `0`. Every table has a sticky `#` position column at the RTL start (right) edge showing 1→15 in the CURRENT display order for any sort (not a rank — a row number); the matrix additionally pins the name column just inside it (offset measured into `--wc26-pos-w`). Medals (🥇🥈🥉) mark each table's canonical top-3 and stay pinned to those players across re-sorts. `wc26.css` holds only RTL alignment overrides (flips MF's Iron-Rule-7 left-align to start/right, loaded AFTER `mf.css`) + content styling; all colors come from shared `--color-*` tokens. Data is a static JS module (no fetch/Supabase). **When v2 reaches the appropriate phase, port WC26 as its own standalone route** reusing v2's design tokens + theme system; keep it RTL, keep the data as a local module unless the betting pool moves to a real backend, and preserve the pinned-medal + non-sortable-matrix behavior.

**Follow-up (2026-07-06): v1 Phase 2 Supabase migration landed** — v1's data layer moved from static `leagues/**` CSV/JSON files to Supabase (read path via `js/data/supabaseLoader.js`, admin write path via `js/admin/supabaseAdmin.js` + Supabase Auth, External Source automation writes directly to Supabase, plus a new Historical Changes admin tab and an analytics POC). Entirely out of scope for this v2 rebuild effort — v2 still reads from the static files/Vite proxy per its existing plan. When v2's own data-layer phase (whichever phase owns `src/data/`) comes up, port the read path (`supabaseLoader.js`'s function-signature contract) and re-evaluate whether v2's admin should also target Supabase directly given v1 no longer writes to `leagues/**` at all post-cutover. See `C:\Users\User\.claude\plans\shiny-cooking-frog.md` (Phase 2 plan) for the full design.

**Follow-up (2026-07-07): query-strategy redesign proposed** — `docs/data-architecture/` (repo root) documents a ground-up rework of v1's Supabase read path (single-RPC bundle + versioned localStorage cache, replacing the current per-page granular queries). Proposed, not yet implemented — see `docs/data-architecture/README.md` for phase status. When v2 reaches its own Supabase data-layer phase, adopt the same `store.js`/bundle design (framework-agnostic by construction) rather than designing a second data layer from scratch.

**Follow-up (2026-07-10): dedicated Sync admin page + named-plans model (v1)** — v1 moved all External Source sync control out of *Edit League → Automatic Sync* into a new top-level **Sync** admin view (`js/admin/syncManager.js`, table **F7**), backed by a new staged file `leagues/sync_settings.json` and a plan-driven scheduler (`sql/external_source_scheduler.sql`: `sync_plans` + `sync_plan_members` + `leagues.source_league_name`). A plan = schedule + member leagues; per-league `ExternalSourceSync` in `league_params.json` is deprecated. Edit League now orders **Settings → Round Editor → Players** (no sync section). Sync-log helpers were extracted to `js/admin/syncLog.js`. **v2 admin stays read-only until Phase 8 — do not build this in v2 now.** When v2's admin phase (owns `src/pages/admin/`) arrives, port the Sync page as its own view + reuse the plan tables; membership single-source-of-truth is the Active-Leagues matrix, and Run Now dispatches via the `trigger_external_source_sync_now_leagues` RPC.

**Follow-up (2026-07-16): analytics gained a privacy-differentiated, timezone-routed identity model (v1)** — `js/analytics.js` + `sql/analytics_poc.sql` moved off the pure zero-correlation design. Routing is by browser timezone only (never IP): Israel visitors (`Asia/Jerusalem`) get a per-visit `session_id` in `sessionStorage` (survives page-to-page nav, wiped on tab close, no cross-visit linkage) so a visit's click sequence can be reconstructed; everyone else stays zero-correlation (no id, nothing written to device for analytics) and carries only a coarse continent `region` (e.g. `Europe`) for regional traffic. New nullable columns `session_id` + `region` (+ CHECK + partial index) and `analytics_summary` gained `total_sessions` + `by_region`. A **passive** transparency notice — NOT a consent banner — was added: `js/render/privacyNotice.js` (self-contained bilingual EN/HE modal) triggered by a `[data-action="privacy"]` link in the **dashboard footer** (`landingPage.js`, the themed `.site-platform-footer` row: "Privacy & Analytics · © Built by Guy Eliyahu"; `pointer-events:none` on `.platform-credit` was the reason an earlier sidebar link wasn't clickable). **Canonical language source:** the modal owns only its own EN/HE text; language STATE and the UK/IL flag control both come from `js/utils/popupLang.js` (`langFlagsHtml()` + get/set/onPopupLangChange, key `shabi-popup-lang`, default English) — the exact same toggle used by every info "?" popup, so switching language anywhere updates everywhere. Do NOT duplicate a second language mechanism in v2. The modal box is themed via the site's `--color-*` tokens (`--color-surface/-text/-border/-accent/-on-accent`), not hardcoded colors. **When v2 builds its own analytics phase, port this hybrid model, the two columns, and the passive notice — do not resurrect the old "no identifier of any kind" design.** Legal rationale (consent vs. notice distinction, timezone≠IP, coarse-region to limit fingerprinting for a small audience) lives in the 2026-07-16 design conversation.

**Follow-up (2026-07-17): analytics gained `admin_user` + a per-visit Sessions view (v1)** — extends the 2026-07-16 model above; the two-route privacy design is unchanged and still binding. (1) New nullable column `admin_user` on `analytics_events` (+ length-only CHECK; no closed set to constrain), set in `js/analytics.js`'s `send()` choke point from `js/admin/auth.js` as `isLoggedIn() ? getUsername().split('@')[0] : null`. It names the site OPERATOR, never a visitor — null on every ordinary visitor's row on BOTH routes — and rides both routes on purpose (an admin abroad is on the global route). Only the email's local part is stored, keeping an addressable identifier out of the table. Note `getUsername()` returns `''`, not null, when logged out, so the empty string must normalise to null. `analytics.js` now statically imports `auth.js` despite its top-level await — the beacon already blocks on `supabaseClient.js` fetching supabase-js from esm.sh, so `getSession()` (a localStorage read, no network for non-admins) costs nothing on top, and a lazy read would leave `admin_user` null on the first pageview of every load. (2) `analytics_summary` gained a `sessions` key: a `sess` CTE grouping `where session_id is not null` (that predicate IS the privacy gate — foreign rows have no id and can never be grouped), capped at 200 visits BEFORE a `cross join lateral` builds each timeline (≤500 events), so payload is bounded regardless of traffic. `clicks_log` additionally selects `session_id`/`region`/`admin_user`. (3) Dashboard: a "Sessions from Israel" section (native `<details>` cards, lazy-rendered on first expand) in the Behavior tab, expanding into the same table as the all-clicks log; plus a Session ID column there showing an 8-char chip or "External · &lt;continent&gt;". Chip colour hashes **`admin_user`, never `session_id`** (all of one admin's visits share a hue; every ordinary visitor shares one neutral), quantised to a 12-spoke wheel so two admins never get near-identical hues. The three log tables were unified behind one `renderLogTable`/`mountFilteredLog` pair, and `css/analytics.css`'s device tints moved from `#id` to `.log-table` (an id can't cover N per-session tables). **When v2 builds its analytics phase, port `admin_user` and the sessions view with the gate intact — grouping is Israel-route-only, foreign events stay unlinked individual rows, and never drop below continent granularity.**

**Follow-up (2026-07-17b): analytics dashboard restructured around the format break (v1)** — the sessions work above made the old tab layout obsolete, and an audit found real value being computed and thrown away. (1) **Format break**: rows collected before the identity model carry no `session_id` AND no `region`; that per-row predicate — never a cutover date, since July 2026 contains both shapes — is now the definition of "legacy". `analytics_summary` gained `scope` ('all'|'new'|'legacy'), `exclude_admin`, and `hide_admin_pages`; **the 2-arg overload had to be dropped explicitly** (`create or replace` cannot change an argument list, and a lingering overload lets PostgREST silently serve unfiltered data). The `ev` CTE split into `evx` (range+scope, operator traffic included) and `ev` (what panels read), so the new `traffic_mix` key can report how much the filter removed. New `analytics_months()` RPC drives a month picker that replaced the rolling 30d/3m/6m/12m windows — those straddled the break and blended the two shapes into one meaningless number. (2) **Internal traffic is filtered ON by default** — every KPI/chart/top-list previously counted the operator's own admin browsing as audience (measured: ~47% of pageviews in range). (3) **Tabs are now Overview / Audience / Content / Journeys / Activity / History**, one question per tab, ordered by resolution (population → cut → session → event). "Traffic Patterns" was dissolved: a day×hour heatmap and a dwell histogram never answered the same question. Revived `by_region` and `transitions` (both computed since forever, never rendered). History is a lazy-fetched frozen archive (`scope:'legacy'`) with no Sessions/Region/composition panels — legacy rows cannot support them. (4) **Legacy data cannot be back-filled to the new format — verified, do not retry**: `region` has zero signal (timezone was never stored, IP never collected); `session_id` is impossible AND would violate the model (the only fingerprint is device+os+browser, which is 95.5% one value); `admin_user` is recoverable only as a ~15% lower bound from `page='admin'` (which proves a login) — the GoTrue auth log cannot extend it, since events split **50/50** around admin activity (the session refreshed for days and never logged out, so "was logged in" ≈ "always"). History therefore uses a **derived display-time filter**, never a backfill, and its label says "at least N". **When v2 builds its analytics phase, port the scope/format-break model and the default-on internal filter; do not resurrect rolling windows across the break, and do not invent identity for legacy rows.**

**Follow-up (2026-07-18): query-strategy redesign IMPLEMENTED + resilience hardening (v1)** — the `docs/data-architecture/` proposal (2026-07-07 follow-up above) landed in v1: single-RPC `get_site_bundle()` + versioned `localStorage` cache via `js/data/store.js`/`bundleMapper.js`, replacing the per-page granular queries (measured on production: cold page loads 8–24 blocking Supabase requests → **1**, warm forward navs → **0**). Applied to cloud Supabase via SQL Editor (`sql/site_bundle.sql`: `site_meta` version counter + statement-level `bump_data_version` triggers + `get_site_bundle` RPC), and the 7 legacy leagues' medal counts were corrected to `1/1/4` (they'd been seeded `0/0/4` while the app reads `?? 1/1/4`). On top of that, a 9-item **resilience pass** (three waves) hardened the read path against flaky-mobile / passive-tab failure modes — all client-side, no further SQL:

- **`js/data/store.js`** — (1) `AbortController` + 10s timeout on every RPC/version-check (a static import or a frozen mobile socket could otherwise hang a page on "Loading…" forever); (2) poison-reset of `_bundlePromise` on failure + a 3s `FAIL_COOLDOWN` so sequential consumers (navbar → page → sidebar) don't stack N×10s; (3) `onVisibleRevalidate(reRenderFn)` — on `visibilitychange→visible`/`pageshow.persisted`, silently version-checks and, only if the data changed OR a previously-failed load can now succeed, refreshes + re-renders in place (scroll preserved) — this is what makes a tab left open for hours **self-heal on return with no manual refresh**, the symptom that triggered the work; trigger is return-to-visible, never a timer, so an actively-viewed tab is never yanked; (4) cross-tab `storage` listener — one tab's refresh is adopted by the others network-free; (5) cache eviction on foreign `schemaVersion` or age > `MAX_AGE` (7 days), **scoped to the `shabi:bundle:v1` key only — never `localStorage.clear()`**, which would wipe analytics' `sessionStorage` session id + display prefs (the 2026-07-16 privacy model depends on that id surviving).
- **`js/compute/crossLeague.js`, `js/render/navigation.js`** — the sibling memos built on top of the bundle (`allLeaguesPromise`, `playerIndexPromise`, `leagueIndexReady`) gained their own poison-reset + a `registerMemoInvalidator` hook so a bundle refresh/heal clears the computed layer too, not just the raw cache.
- **`js/render/heroBanner.js` + `js/render/landingPage.js`** — the `no-store` banner-config fetch got a 6s timeout and was decoupled from the `Promise.all` that gated the league list (a stuck banner used to freeze the whole landing page); its loading shimmer now ends on a `dataReady` signal, order-independent.
- **`js/data/supabaseClient.js` + `js/vendor/`** — supabase-js is now **vendored** (`js/vendor/supabase-js@2.110.7.mjs`, a single self-contained esbuild bundle) instead of imported from `https://esm.sh/@supabase/supabase-js@2`. That import sat on the critical path of every page with no possible timeout (you can't `AbortController` a static import) and leaked every visitor's IP to a third party — at odds with the privacy notice's "we do not store IP addresses". See `js/vendor/README.md` for the reproducible build command.

**When v2 reaches its Supabase data-layer phase, port `store.js` WITH this hardening baked in — the timeout/poison-reset/visibility-revalidate/cross-tab/eviction behaviors are not optional polish, each fixes a specific failure a real run hit — and vendor supabase-js from the start rather than importing it from a CDN.** Full design + the browser-verified before/after in `docs/data-architecture/` (see `README.md` phase status and `04-performance-budget.md`).

**Follow-up (2026-07-21): static-asset cache-busting is a cutover driver — v1 cannot be fully fixed, v2/Vite already is.** A user reported seeing a ~4-day-stale build after repeated refreshes. Investigation (curl + Playwright, prior session) isolated it to **static code files only** (CSS/JS) — the DB read path is queried live on every visit and is unaffected; "old data" is old *code* rendering/caching data the old way. Findings: `golan.me.uk` = GitHub Pages behind Fastly, `Cache-Control: max-age=600` (GitHub-enforced, unchangeable) + ETag/Last-Modified, **no Service Worker, no Cloudflare**. So for a normal browser every file re-validates within 10 min; the 4-day staleness points at a **stuck intermediate proxy** (ISP/corporate) that ignores `max-age` and serves its own copy regardless of ETag — which repeated in-browser refreshes can't clear. Cache-busting (a URL change → the proxy treats it as a new resource) is the only client-independent cure. Two Israeli sports sites confirm the technique is universal but content-hash is just *one* method: ONE (`one.co.il`) uses manual per-file `?v=22` + `immutable` (and has an un-busted `mini-player.js` that can stick for a year); Sport5 uses one global `?SiteVersion=11.59` on every file.

**The v1 structural limit — why a stop-gap can only ever be half a fix.** v1 is ES modules with *relative* imports inside the code (e.g. `analyticsPage.js` does `import '../data/supabaseClient.js'`). Appending `?v=` to an entry file's URL busts **only that file** — the query does not propagate to a sibling resolved relatively, so the entire internal import graph is still pulled from the stale drawer. Therefore manual query-versioning on v1 can cover only what a page's HTML names directly:
- **CSS — fully bustable.** Every stylesheet is a flat `<link>` with no import chain, so a single stamp on every `<link>` busts the whole CSS surface at once, even past a stuck proxy. v1 *already* does this ad-hoc and inconsistently: `components.css?v=3`, `navigation.css?v=2`, `player-general.css?v=11` — hand-bumped per-file counters (the ONE `mini-player.js` failure mode: easy to forget a file). A single unified `ASSET_VERSION` on all links would make it consistent; its value is real but modest (the volatile CSS files are already stamped).
- **JS — effectively *not* bustable *by hand*.** The page-level entry `<script type="module">` blocks are trivial 2–4 line bootstraps (e.g. analytics.html: import `renderAnalyticsPage` + `themePicker`, call three functions). Stamping the entry busts only the bootstrap; every module of real logic (`store.js`, `supabaseClient.js`, the render graph) lives in the un-reachable relative-import graph and stays on the 10-min / stuck-proxy behavior. Against the exact scenario that triggered this (a stuck proxy serving stale JS), a manual entry stamp gives a *misleading half-fix*: fresh CSS + fresh bootstrap pulling stale modules. Busting the JS graph therefore *requires a build step* — an importmap doesn't intercept relative paths.

**Two real fixes now exist — one in v1, one native to v2.**

*v1 (implemented 2026-07-21):* `scripts/build-v1.cjs` + a root `package.json`/`npm run build` add an **esbuild** content-hash build to v1 without touching its source-served dev workflow. It extracts each page's inline `<script type="module">` to an entry, bundles+splits the whole graph into `/assets/build/<name>-<hash>.js` (+ shared `chunk-<hash>.js`), content-hashes every CSS file **in dependency order** (rewriting `@import url("../base/base.css")` → the hashed name — the CSS analog of the import-graph problem), strips the ad-hoc `?v=N`, and rewrites each page's `<link>`/entry `<script>` to the hashed URLs → `dist/` (a deployable mirror; copies `CNAME`, writes `.nojekyll`). Browser-verified via Playwright MCP against cloud data: landing/leagueTable/playerLeague/WC26(RTL)/admin all render, **0 console errors, 0 page-level horizontal overflow**, the admin bundle's nested dynamic `import()` resolves to a hashed chunk, and a change to a deep module (`landingPage.js`) flips its entry bundle's hash while untouched files keep theirs. A dormant `.github/workflows/deploy-pages.yml` (manual-dispatch until Pages Source is switched to "GitHub Actions") deploys it. **Cost:** v1 now carries a real build step — exactly the machinery v2 provides for free — bolted onto v1's tree, and it re-fingerprints the whole graph on any change (no per-file cache reuse beyond what content-hashing gives).

*v2/Vite (native, verified 2026-07-21):* the Vite build content-hashes the *entire* graph, rewrites every import, and emits HTML pointing at the hashed names — continuously, as part of the normal build, with no separate bolt-on script to maintain. Confirmed by an actual `npm run build`: `v2/vite.config.js` has **no `output.entryFileNames`/`assetFileNames`/`chunkFileNames` overrides**, so Rollup's hashing defaults apply (`landing-<hash>.js`, `leagueTable-<hash>.js`, `designCatalogue-<hash>.css`, referenced from the emitted HTML). Do **not** add an `output` filename override that strips the hash. **Cutover argument, restated accurately:** the cache-staleness bug is now *solved in both*, but in v1 it costs an extra build pipeline maintained by hand on legacy source; in v2 it is a free property of the build the rebuild already needs. When v1 is retired at cutover, `scripts/build-v1.cjs` + the root `package.json`/`deploy-pages.yml` retire with it.

## Phase 2 — primitive coverage of v1 styles

Each primitive subsumes a slice of `css/components.css` / `css/admin.css`. Components in Phase 3 will compose these primitives, fully replacing the v1 rules.

| v1 class | v2 primitive | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `.flag`, `.flag-title` | `Flag` (sizes `md` / `lg`) | Em-based heights replace v1's fixed `16px` / `24px`. |
| `.medal`, `.medal-gold`/`silver`/`bronze` | `Badge.--circle` + `--gold/silver/bronze` | Same colour tokens. |
| `.status-pill`, `.status-running`, `.status-completed` | `Pill.--running/completed` | |
| `.league-type-pill`, `.type-doubling/regular/ubc` | `Pill.--doubling/regular/ubc` + `.--uppercase` | Uppercase opt-in (v1 was always uppercase). |
| `.retired-badge` | `Pill` (composition decision — see Phase 3) | |
| `.img-export-btn`, `.admin-button-toggle`, `.matchup-show-all-btn` | `Button.--primary` / `Button.--icon` / `Button.--pill` | |
| `.floating-btn-tooltip` (admin/theme picker hover labels) | `Tooltip` | |
| `.matchup-search-input` + various admin inputs | `FormField` (input, select, textarea variants) | |
| `td.player-cell a`, `.landing-table a`, `.matchup-table a` | `Link.--quiet` | Same "underline on hover only" behaviour. **Link-cell weight contract (2026-06-18):** every table link cell must be 600. In v1 the weight came from the cell (`td.player-cell { font-weight: 600 }`); in v2 `.link--quiet` itself now pins `font-weight: var(--fw-subheading)` so a quiet table link can never inherit body weight even outside a `player-cell`. Both belt and braces. |
| `td.league-cell a` (C1/C2 League column) | `Link.--strong` | The clickable League column on the player-general tables must read like the OPPONENT cell (semibold + quiet link + accent hover). v1 achieves this by sharing the `td.player-cell`/`td.league-cell` rule group; v2's `Link.--strong` already bundles `color: text` + `--fw-subheading` + hover-accent, so the league `<a>` uses `--strong`. As of 2026-06-18 `--quiet` also carries `--fw-subheading`, so either variant is now safe for a league link — neither renders at regular weight. See the §"Link-cell weight contract (ALL formats)" in `docs/TABLE-DESIGN.md`. |
| `.admin-welcome-avatar` | `Avatar` (initials variant, size `md`) | Replaces the bespoke gradient circle. |
