## Bug fixes during rebuild

Bug fixes applied to v1 during the rebuild MUST be re-applied to the corresponding v2 destination. Track them here:

| Date | v1 commit | v1 file | v2 status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-08-12 | (staged) | **NEW: real-time match ingestion from External Source e-mail reports, with two new admin tables F8 + F9 (feature addition, logged here because it MUST be re-applied to v2).** Files: NEW `sql/mail_sync.sql`, `js/admin/mailSync.js`, `tools/mail-sync/Code.gs`, `docs/ui-demos/mail-sync-demo.html`; modified `js/admin/syncManager.js`, `css/admin.css`, `docs/TABLE-DESIGN.md`. **What it is:** the source mails a report per finished match to a private mailbox; an automation there parses the *subject line only* and calls `submit_match_report(token, payload)` — the single function `anon` may execute, every table RLS-closed with no policies, the shared secret stored only as a SHA-256 hash. **Three load-bearing facts that must not be re-derived:** (1) **applying a report is an UPDATE of an existing unplayed fixture, never an INSERT** — `matches` already holds the full round-robin (played + unplayed), which is also the only reason league inference is possible at all: a candidate league is one already holding this pairing as an open fixture. A v2 port that assumes INSERT breaks on `unique (league_id, round, player_a, player_b)` or silently duplicates. (2) **orientation** — the report's A/B may be the fixture's reverse; `mail_candidate_leagues` returns a `swapped` flag and `mail_apply_report` swaps names, scores, PR **and** luck together. This is the same class of bug the manual-overrides key orientation already cost this project once. (3) **`mail_apply_report` re-runs the candidate scan server-side before writing** and never trusts the stored candidate list — an admin can sit on the F8 screen while a CSV import or an override lands underneath. **Equivalence rule (user-specified):** a mail-applied match is a CSV-applied match — override-covered pairings are excluded from candidacy exactly as `csvValidation.js` excludes them from "N updates"; `match_history.source` is written `'csv'`; and the audit batch uses `topic`/`specific`/`icon` byte-identical to what `scripts/sync-source.js` writes, with only the free-text `detail` naming the match. Provenance lives in `match_reports`, which is what F9 reads. **Verified live (MCP, :8090, local Docker Supabase):** 10 reports pushed through the anon RPC — 6 auto-applied, 2 held in F8 with 2 and 3 candidates, a replay swallowed as `duplicate`, a bad token rejected; a deliberately reversed report landed with scores and PR on the correct sides; Apply in the UI moved a row to F9 with the `ADMIN` pill; the matches appear in `league.html?tab=matches` with `played_at` dates; Historical Changes shows one Details/Undo entry per match. | ◯ pending — v2 has **no** admin yet (Phase 8) and no `FormTable` yet (Phase 5), so there is no destination to port into today. SQL is version-agnostic and already applies to both. | **v2:** the SQL layer ports unchanged — it is the contract, and the three facts above live in it deliberately rather than in the UI. Destinations: `js/admin/mailSync.js` → `src/pages/admin/mailSync.js` (Phase 8); F8/F9 → `FormTable` presets `src/tables/presets/F8_unassignedEmailMatches.js` + `F9_mailAutomatedMatches.js` (Phase 5). **Build F8 as a real `FormTable` mount from the start** — v1 hand-built the chrome to match its six sibling tables pending Phase 8, which is a v1-legacy reason with no v2 equivalent. F8 = Display cells + one Edit-mode `<select>` (options = the server-computed candidate list) + one Action cell; F9 = Display only. **`FormTable` needs a `stickyCols: 2` option** — F8/F9 pin Player A **and** Player B (a match row's identity is the pair; pinning only column 1 leaves a score against one name). v1 added this as the `ff-sticky-2` FF variant (class on the table, offset from a JS-measured `--ff-col1-w`) deliberately spelled like SF's existing `sf-sticky-N` + `--sf-col1-w`; in v2 it belongs in `FormTable` as a `stickyCols` arg, exactly as `mountSFTable` already exposes it. Two invariants. **(a) Measure after the row images load, not on first layout** — `.flag` is `height: 1em; width: auto`, so an unloaded flag contributes 0 width: measured on F8, th1 is 117.67px before the flags land and 131.33px after, exactly one 13.67px flag, parking column 2 fourteen pixels inside column 1 permanently. Watch an element that reflows when the images do (the header cell); the wrap never resizes, and a one-shot rAF does not help because **pinning does not itself change column 1's width** (verified identical with and without the class). SF's `requestAnimationFrame` + `ResizeObserver(table)` shape is only safe where cells carry no images — do not copy it blindly into a table with flags. **(b)** the scroll shadow moves to the **last** pinned column and is cancelled on the earlier ones, or a seam is drawn between columns that are pinned together. **F9 must sort by the value its Date column shows (`played_at`), not by arrival** — the two diverge on forwarded and admin-assigned reports, and a table sorted by a column it does not display reads as unsorted. **Do not draw a custom `<select>` arrow.** The v1 canon (`ff.css`) styles the FF select with em-based gutter only and keeps the native arrow; an early draft of this feature shipped `appearance:none` with a hardcoded `stroke='%23888'`, which would have painted a grey arrow on vegas' deep red and on dark. In v2 this is additionally a Stylelint violation (literal colour outside `tokens/`/`themes/`) — the linter would have caught it, which is an argument for the v2 rule, not against it. The automation (`tools/mail-sync/Code.gs`) is not v1 or v2 code at all — it runs in a third-party mailbox and is version-independent; keep it where it is and do not port it into `src/`. |
| 2026-08-02 | (working tree) | **Player profile ▸ Matches: the league-type filter is now a section-wide pill bar, and PR stops being an offerable metric where no league records it.** Files: `js/render/playerGeneralPage.js` (`renderMatchHistory`), `css/player-general.css`, `player.html` (`player-general.css?v=13`). **(A) Filter** — the `Type:` `<select>` that lived inside the chart's `.dash-controls` row governed the chart only in appearance while actually filtering the C2 table too, and it was the last league-type filter on the page still rendered as a dropdown (G4 Leagues had already moved to pills). Replaced with the shared `mountPillTabs` bar (`js/render/subTabs.js`, `ALL_TYPES_TAB` + only the types the player actually played, canonical order, `league-type-pill type-*` styling) mounted in a new `.pg-matches-filter` above the chart card, so its scope reads as what it is: one filter for the whole section — chart AND table. **(B) PR metric relevance** — REGULAR leagues record no PR (`compute/leagueTypes.js` `showPR: false`), yet rows arriving from the external-source import can still carry a `prSelf` number, which the C2 table correctly printed as "—" while the bar chart happily plotted it and folded it into the moving average. Now: `typeHasPR()` reads `getLeagueConfig().showPR` (single source, no second hardcoded list); the chart feed nulls `prSelf`/`prOpp` on rows whose league type records none, so under ALL a REGULAR match keeps its slot and its match number but draws no bar; and the Metric control (label + select, newly wrapped in `.pg-metric-ctl` so the pair hides as one) drops the PR option whenever no PR-recording type is in view — a REGULAR-only player's ALL included — leaving Luck, and since a one-option select is not a choice the control hides entirely. The player's own last metric pick is remembered and restored the moment a PR-recording type is back in view. Verified live (MCP, 8090) across all four states: mixed ALL, mixed→REGULAR, mixed→DOUBLING, REGULAR-only ALL (REGULAR leagues were simulated by rewriting `league_type` in the mocked `rpc/get_site_bundle` response — the local DB has none). | ◯ pending — v2 destination `v2/src/pages/player/` (Matches tab). v2 already has `FilterPill`/`TypePill` components; the rule "PR is a property of the league type, not of the row" must land in the v2 chart feed too, not only in the table formatter. |
| 2026-08-02 | (working tree) | **Export Image — the BMAB title badge (M2/G0/A1…) rendered oversized: 4px wider and 4px taller than on the live page, floating above the championship badge (NC/WC) beside it.** File: `js/utils/exportTableImage.js` (both clone paths — the WhatsApp fixed-frame `exportWhatsAppImage` and the plain `exportTableImage`). **Root cause:** on the live page `.title-abbr` draws its outline as `box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 1.5px currentColor` *precisely so it consumes no layout space* — the outlined BMAB pill and the gradient championship pill then have byte-identical boxes and share one baseline. html2canvas cannot render an inset shadow (verified against html2canvas-pro 1.5.11: it paints the whole pill in the shadow colour with the glyphs knocked out — **the fork did not fix this**, so the clone must substitute something), and the substitute was a real `border: 1.5px solid currentColor` on the BMAB pills only. A real border occupies layout, so the pill grew in **both** dimensions; with `vertical-align: text-bottom` pinning the bottom edge the extra height grows upward, which is what made it float. **Rejected alternatives, both measured:** subtracting the border from the padding lands 1px off (browsers round border widths to whole device pixels — 1.5px renders as 1px at DPR 1) and collapses entirely in the WhatsApp frame, where the fitted table font drives `0.12em` of padding below the border width; `outline` + negative `outline-offset` has the right zero-layout geometry but html2canvas-pro **does not render outlines at all** (rendered pill came out frameless). **Fix:** `normaliseTitleBadgesForExport(root)` gives each BMAB pill `position: relative` and appends an **absolutely-positioned overlay `<span>`** (`inset:0; border:1.5px solid currentColor; border-radius:inherit; box-sizing:border-box`). Out of flow → the pill keeps exactly its live width, height and baseline; html2canvas gets a plain bordered box it renders correctly. `inset:0` resolves against the pill's padding box, which — the pill having no border — is its outer edge, so the frame lands exactly where the shadow was. **Championship pills are no longer touched at all** (real gradient background, no inset shadow → already faithful); leaving both variants at live geometry is what keeps them matched. Note the `position: relative` is set **unconditionally** — the clone is still detached when the helper runs, so `getComputedStyle` has nothing to test against. **Verified:** pill-height÷font-size is 1.2353 live vs 1.2369 in the export clone, width-ratio 2.1789 vs 2.1783 (~0.1%, sub-pixel rounding at the larger pinned export font); NC and M2 byte-identical in the clone; overlay rect exactly equals the pill rect. | ◯ pending — Phase 6 (`v2/src/components/ExportTableImage/exportTableImage.js`), which currently has none of the v1 badge handling | **v2:** the 2026-05-29 row below already says Phase 6 should copy `js/utils/exportTableImage.js` verbatim — copy it **after** this fix, i.e. take `normaliseTitleBadgesForExport` with it. Invariants: (a) never substitute a layout-occupying `border` for the inset outline — the pill must keep its live box, so draw the frame with an out-of-flow overlay; (b) do not touch the championship variant; (c) `outline` is not an option while html2canvas-pro is the rasteriser. Alternative if v2 prefers a source-level fix: give `.title-abbr-champ::before` `border-radius: inherit`, drop the `overflow: hidden` it needs today, and move the live `.title-abbr` outline to a real border with the padding reduced once in CSS — then live and export are literally the same box and no clone patch is needed at all. That touches the production page, so it is a v2-greenfield decision, not a v1 change. **Superseded note:** an earlier same-day attempt gave the championship pill a matching transparent border; it aligned the two pills with each other but left both 2px oversized against the live page — do not port that shape. |

| 2026-07-28 | (working tree) | **Luck metric replaced (Bayesian "Luck Confidence" D) + single-source popup content + NEW PM table format + "Explanation and Maths" tool.** Files: NEW `js/compute/luckConfidence.js`, NEW `js/render/luckContribCurve.js`, NEW `js/data/popupContent.js`, NEW `table-lab/formats/pm/{mount.js,pm.css}`, NEW `explanation-and-maths.html` + `tools/START_EXPLANATION_AND_MATHS.bat`; changed `js/compute/{championshipPredictor.js,allTimeRankings.js}`, `js/render/{landingPage.js,dashboardPage.js}`, `css/dashboard.css`, `docs/TABLE-DESIGN.md`. **(1) Luck metric.** The old signed-luck score (`(1/n)Σrᵢ·\|rᵢ\|`) and the z-score Luck Percentile (`Φ((AW−EW)/√Var)`) are BOTH replaced by one Bayesian metric **D = 100·P(θ>0\|data)**: a latent per-match luck shift θ~Normal(0,s²) (`LUCK_PRIOR_S=1.0`), each match multiplying the prior by `contribᵢ(θ)=σ(logit(pᵢ)+θ)` on a win / `1−that` on a loss, D = posterior mass above θ=0 via grid integration over θ∈[−6,6] step 0.02. It self-regularises (a tiny/all-win sample is pulled off the 0/100 boundary toward 50 by the prior, so no "unstable sample <15 games" cutoff is needed). Bell-adapted bands (equal z-steps `Z_CUTS=[0.2,0.55,0.9,1.5]`→`LUCK_DIST_CUTS≈[7.9,20.9,31.6,43.3]` on \|D−50\|), same five labels as the PR-difference scale. `luckConfidenceStats` is a drop-in for the old `luckPercentileStats` (returns `.percentile`=round(D)); wired into `allTimeRankings.js` + `landingPage.js`. **(2) Single-source popup content.** NEW `js/data/popupContent.js` is now the ONE source for every "?" popup's bilingual body; `dashboardPage.js` (4 popups via `popupLangBlocks`) + `landingPage.js` (luck popup via `getPopup`) build their `.popup-lang-en/he` blocks from it. **(3) PM (PR Matrix) table format** — see the TABLE-DESIGN ▸ PM entry: `pmTableHtml(config)` string-builder unifies the PR Win-Probability Table (matrix) + Table Validation (list); `prProbabilityTableHtml` + `buildExplanationTableHtml` route through it; `.pr-prob-*`/`.corr-band-table-scroll` CSS retired in favour of `.pm-*`. | ◯ pending — v2 has no stats-explanation surface yet (read-only until admin phase) | **v2:** (a) port `luckConfidence.js` verbatim as the ONLY luck metric — do NOT reintroduce signed-luck or the z-score percentile; both had the all-win→0/100 pinning + small-sample trap that D fixes by construction. Keep D's drop-in `.percentile` field so ranking tables need no special-casing, and drop the `unstableSample`/`<15 games` struck-through rendering (D folds sample size in). (b) Build the "?" popups from a single bilingual content module like `popupContent.js` from day one — never inline popup copy in two render files again. (c) Port PM as a `PRMatrix` primitive (string-first is fine; both consumers inject HTML) over v2 table tokens, serving both the matrix and list shapes; carry the doubling-only caption note. (d) The standalone `explanation-and-maths.html` tool imports the SAME `popupContent.js` + `pmTableHtml` — keep that shared-source arrangement so a copy edit updates tool and app together. |

| 2026-07-17 | (working tree) | **Manual-override date + technical-result bugs, root-caused to two hand-copied reconcile twins, then de-duplicated into one shared module.** Files: NEW `js/data/matchHistoryReconcile.js`, NEW `sql/manual_overrides_edited_at.sql`; changed `js/admin/supabaseAdmin.js`, `scripts/sync-source.js`, `js/data/supabaseLoader.js`, `js/compute/matchHistory.js`, `js/presets/playerMatchHistoryPreset.js`, `sql/supabase_schema.sql`. **Symptoms (from a screenshot):** a technical WIN rendered `Luck 0.00` + a real `1-0` score, and its date read as the last sync (16/07) rather than the admin's manual edit. **Root cause:** the match_history reconcile existed as TWO copies — `reconcileMatchHistory` (admin publish, `supabaseAdmin.js`) and `reconcileMatchHistoryInSupabase` (sync job, `sync-source.js`) — that silently diverged in THREE places: (a) override date — admin kept an unchanged override's date, sync stamped `now` every run (redating every manual edit + re-upserting it, firing the audit trigger on untouched rows); (b) `not_played` — admin `continue`d (leaving a stale CSV row that the read-side merge resurrected), sync wrote a `luck:0` row (same resurrection, as 0-0); (c) `changedKeys` tracking. | 2026-07-16 | (working tree) | **Audit-log batching: one Publish = one Historical row + delta writes kill the ghost fan-out.** NEW `js/admin/render/changeVocabulary.js`, NEW `sql/audit_batching.sql`; changed `js/admin/render/adminPage.js`, `js/admin/render/historicalChanges.js`, `js/admin/supabaseAdmin.js`, `js/admin/stagingStore.js`, `css/admin.css`. **Problem:** every admin write went through unconditional `upsert`-of-the-whole-table (`bulkImportCSV`/`syncOverrides`/`syncPlayersMetadata`), and `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` fires the per-row audit + `updated_at` triggers on EVERY row — so editing one match wrote ~300 audit rows (measured: 264 no-op "ghost" UPDATEs + 36 real, in one 312-row publish). Historical Changes then listed all 300+ as separate entries. **Fix, three layers:** **(1) Single-source label vocabulary** — `changeVocabulary.js` owns the topic taxonomy (League→the site league glyph from `tabIcons.js`, Player→👤, Settings→⚙️), the per-category secondary icon+text (`CATEGORY_TAXONOMY`), intent rank (`CATEGORY_RANK`), and the ONE formatter `renderChangeLabel({topic,subject,icon,text,detail})` → `{topicIcon} Topic · **subject** · {specificIcon} specific — detail`. Both Pending (`adminPage.js` `renderCategoryLabel`, replacing the old local `CATEGORY_META`/`renderLabel`) and Historical funnel through it, so the two views can't drift. **(2) Delta writes** — the three sync fns now fetch existing rows with all comparable columns and UPSERT only rows that are new or genuinely changed (`sameMatchRow`/`sameOverrideRow`/`samePlayerRow` + `numEq` for PostgREST numeric-as-string); likewise `updateSyncSettings` skips per-league `source_league_name` writes whose value is unchanged (a single-league sync-name edit no longer no-op-updates all the others). Verified on Docker: re-importing an identical CSV writes 0 rows; changing one score writes exactly 1. **(3) Batching** — `sql/audit_batching.sql` adds `audit_batches` + `audit_log.batch_id`, backfills existing rows into logical batches by clustering on `(changed_by, time-island gap≤3s, SUBJECT)` — subject = league_id for league-scoped tables, `'players'`/`'landing'` for the rest — so an automation run that touched X leagues splits into X per-league rows (not one lump), while a league's own fan-out (matches + overrides + match_history + last_updated bump) stays together. Each batch's headline comes from a diff+table-role classifier (`audit_row_intent` + `audit_is_noop`); `audit_batch_summary` exposes primary/derived/ghost counts. Live: `publishAll` batches **per logical unit** (a staged `group`, or each ungrouped change) via a per-unit `current_max_audit_id` watermark + `finalize_publish_batch(intent, afterId)` — so editing settings of X different leagues in one Publish yields X separate Historical rows (matching their X Pending rows), while a create-league group keeps its players as related rows under one row. Delta writes mean no-op ghost rows are never generated in the first place — on the admin path (`bulkImportCSV`/`syncOverrides`/`syncPlayersMetadata`/`updateSyncSettings`) AND the automation script `scripts/sync-source.js` (`writeMatchesToSupabase` + `reconcileMatchHistoryInSupabase` now upsert only changed rows and only bump `last_updated` when matches actually changed). Existing ghosts are hard-pruned (migration §7, active — `audit_is_noop` rows deleted, then empty batches). The Historical row shows a `+N related changes` count (valid rows beyond the headline), renders each field change as a numbered `field / before / after` line (missing/created/deleted → `-`), and never surfaces ghosts. Historical now renders ONE row per batch; ghosts (old==new bar `updated_at`/`last_updated`) are hidden, valid derived rows (match_history reconcile) lazy-load under **Details**. Grouped **Undo** (`restore_batch`) / **✕** (`restore_and_delete_batch`) revert the whole batch atomically to the pre-publish state. | 2026-07-14 | (staged) | **Sidebar section deep-links (Records ▸ sub-menu, Leaders, Players) landed at the wrong scroll position on every click AFTER the first one.** File: `js/render/siteSidebar.js` (`wireInteractions`). **Root cause:** these links are plain `<a href="index.html?tab=records#records-pr">`. The FIRST click comes from a different URL, so the browser does a full document load, `landingPage.js`'s render path runs, and its topbar-aware `scrollToClearingTopbar()` lands the section correctly. But that same path strips the hash from the URL bar (`history.replaceState`, deliberate — see the comment there), so the address becomes `index.html?tab=records`. A SECOND click on any Records link now differs from the current URL **only in the fragment** → the browser performs a same-document fragment navigation: no reload, `renderLandingPage()` never runs again, `scrollToClearingTopbar` is never called, and the browser's own anchor jump parks the section flush at viewport top — with zero awareness of the fixed topbar. Measured live (MCP): the landing scroll overshoots by exactly `topbar height + --space-md` — 80px at 390px wide (64px `.site-topbar` + 16px gap; the section heading ends up fully hidden under the topbar) and 16px at 1920px (no fixed topbar on desktop, so only the breathing-room gap is lost). Nothing caught it: no `hashchange` listener on the landing page, and the records sections deliberately carry no `scroll-margin-top` (see the comment at `css/index-dashboard.css` ~L1234 — the JS measurement was meant to be the single dynamic source of truth, but the repeat-click path had no source of truth at all). **Fix — made CANONICAL rather than patched per-surface, at the user's explicit instruction:** `js/utils/scrollOffset.js` gained `installSectionLinkScroll()`, ONE delegated `document`-level click listener that owns every same-page section link site-wide. A click is taken over only when it's a plain left click (not already `defaultPrevented`, no modifier/new-tab intent), the link's `pathname` AND `search` both equal the current document's, and the `#id` resolves; then it `preventDefault()`s, re-strips the hash, closes any nav flyout that was pinned open to show the link, and scrolls via `scrollToClearingTopbar(target, {behavior: prefersReducedMotion() ? 'auto' : 'smooth'})`. Everything else falls through to a normal navigation (where the load-time render path already scrolls correctly). It is installed from **`mountTopbar()`** (`js/render/topbar.js`) — the module that mounts the fixed chrome is the one that installs the compensation for it, so no surface can mount the topbar and forget to wire this; every page (public via `siteSidebar.js`, admin via `adminPage.js`/`adminSidebar.js`) gets it for free, and no sidebar/page-local click handler exists any more. Comparing `search` is also what keeps it safe against hidden tab panels: `appTabs.js`'s `syncUrl()` mirrors the active tab into `?tab=` on every switch, so an identical `search` guarantees the target sits in the VISIBLE panel. **Second bug, same family, found by testing Players → Records-PR (a CROSS-tab click, which is a full reload, not the same-doc case above):** the landing page's own load-time deep-link scroll (`js/render/landingPage.js`) overshot by ~236px intermittently. Root cause is a race — it gated the scroll on the `buildAllTimeRankings` data promises resolving, but `renderAchievementsSection`/`renderPRLeadersSection`/etc. build their (tall) DOM in a LATER microtask than the promise they await, so any section ABOVE the target could still grow after the one-shot scroll fired, pushing the target down with nothing to re-correct it. When rankings resolved from cache (fast) the scroll won the race and landed right; via the Players tab it lost. **Fix — generalised, not a bigger gate:** new `scrollToClearingTopbarSettled(target, {settleMs, maxMs})` in `scrollOffset.js` stops trying to PREDICT when layout is done and instead OBSERVES it — scroll now, then re-assert via a `ResizeObserver` on `document.documentElement` on every height change until it stays quiet for `settleMs` (400ms) or `maxMs` (3s) elapses or the user scrolls (wheel/touch/nav-key cancels, so we never yank the viewport out from under them; our own instant scrollTo emits none of those events). landingPage's deep-link path now calls it (and the old `buildAllTimeRankings` pre-gate + its now-unused `needsAsyncWait` branch are deleted — the observer covers async table growth AND late images/fonts for free). Verified live (MCP, both widths): Players → Records-PR now lands at targetTop 16 (desktop) / 80 (mobile), scrollY 1650/1551, vs the pre-fix 252. **Same pass, same root cause, admin side:** `js/admin/msgScroll.js`'s `revealAtTop()` (used by sync **Run Now** to put the Progress card at the top) had the mirror-image version of the bug — `scrollIntoView({block:'start'})` plus a CSS `scroll-margin-top: var(--space-lg)` (24px) on `.sync-stages-panel`. `block:'start'` aligns to the top of the SCROLLPORT, which a `position:fixed` topbar overlaps without shrinking, and admin.html mounts the very same 64px `.site-topbar` — so the card's top 40px (its heading) sat under the bar whenever the sidebar was closed, i.e. always on mobile. `revealAtTop` now delegates to `scrollToClearingTopbar`; the `scroll-margin-top` rule is deleted (with a comment saying why it must not come back). `revealMsg()`'s "is it on screen" test was `rect.top < 0`, which counts a message tucked under the topbar as visible — now `rect.top < measureFixedTopOffset()`. Verified live (MCP, 390×844 + 1920×945): repeat clicks land at the identical `scrollY` as the first-click/page-load path (1565 mobile / 1792 desktop, gap 16px below the bar), cross-section jumps (PR → Match, PR → League) work with no reload, the cross-tab Leaders link still does a real navigation, flyouts unpin, and the console is clean. Note discovered while testing: the topbar is NOT mobile-only — `body.site-sidebar-closed .site-topbar { display: flex }` means it appears on desktop too the moment the sidebar is collapsed, which is exactly why the offset must be measured at click time and can never be a constant. | ◯ pending — v2's sidebar/landing/admin destinations (Phase 2/3/8) not built yet. | **v2:** a section deep-link that may be clicked while already on its own page is NOT just an `<a href="#id">` — the browser's native anchor jump ignores fixed chrome, and any load-time-only scroll correction silently stops applying the moment the click becomes a same-document fragment navigation. Port this as ONE global handler installed alongside the fixed chrome (not per-surface wiring), and route every programmatic "bring this section into view" through the same shared `scrollToClearingTopbar` (see the 2026-07-07 row below — same utility, same rule). When the target lands on a page whose content above it is still filling in async (records tables, etc.), use the observe-and-re-assert variant (`scrollToClearingTopbarSettled`) rather than gating a one-shot scroll on "the data promise resolved" — the DOM build happens in a later microtask than the promise, so a data-gate still races the layout. Three anti-patterns to keep out of v2 by construction, all of which produced real bugs here: `scrollIntoView({block:'start'})` on a page with fixed top chrome; a CSS `scroll-margin-top` constant standing in for that chrome's height; and a one-shot scroll into async-growing content gated on a data promise instead of observing layout. `block:'nearest'` inside a scrollable dropdown/listbox is unrelated and fine. If v2 does want the CSS route, the chrome's height must be published as a live CSS var, and it must account for the chrome not always being present. |
| 2026-07-11 | (staged) | **B2 Historical view: update points now carry TIME, not just date.** `js/compute/matchHistory.js` + `js/render/dashboardPage.js`. **Root cause:** `getUpdateDates()` truncated `updatedAt` to `slice(0,10)` (day precision) and `drawHistTable` used `dateValue + 'T23:59:59Z'` as the cutoff — so two distinct same-day update points (e.g. 09:48 and 17:39) collapsed into one "2026-07-09" bucket and the time was lost. **Fix:** new `getUpdatePoints(history)` groups by MINUTE (collapses a single sync's sub-second write spread) and returns `{value: <exact latest ISO in that minute>, label: <"9 Jul 2026, 17:39">}` newest-first; `getMatchesAsOf` is called with the exact timestamp (no end-of-day rounding). `getUpdateDates` kept (unused in v1 now) to avoid breaking any other importer. **Also (same session) — not-yet-played players must not be dropped from historical/as-of views:** (1) B2 (`drawHistTable`) stays a medal-only preview but dropped the `&& games > 0` clause (now `rank <= medalLimit && player !== 'Bye'`), so a not-yet-played player who ranks within the medals still shows. (2) The full league table (D, `js/render/leaguePage.js`) historical `?asof=` path used to rebuild `viewPlayers` from only the players appearing in the as-of matches — dropping anyone who hadn't played yet. Fixed to keep `viewPlayers = allPlayers` (the full roster, same as CURRENT); only the MATCHES are filtered to the cutoff, so 0-game players are still ranked. Pairs with the DB-side fix that made this data trustworthy: dropped the blanket `set_updated_at` BEFORE UPDATE trigger on `match_history` (it stamped `now()` on every reconcile upsert, collapsing all history) — see `sql/supabase_schema.sql`. | ◯ pending | **v2:** `v2/src/data/matchHistory.js` mirrors the v1 `getUpdateDates` day-precision logic and has unit tests (`v2/tests/unit/matchHistory.test.js`) — port the minute-precision `getUpdatePoints` + exact-timestamp cutoff, and ensure the v2 Supabase schema does NOT apply a generic `updated_at` trigger to `match_history` (it's domain data managed by reconcile). |

| 2026-08-02 | (staged) | **NEW full-screen loading screen replaces both the old `.logo-splash` breathe overlay AND the per-page `<div class="loading">Loading…</div>` placeholders (feature addition, logged here because it MUST be re-applied to v2).** Files: NEW `css/splash.css`, `js/utils/splashConfig.js`, `assets/splash/splash-config.json`, `splash-poc.html` (editor) + `tools/START_SPLASH_EDITOR.bat`; REWRITTEN `js/utils/splash.js`; modified all five page HTMLs (`index`, `league_table`, `league`, `player_league`, `player` — added the `<link>`, the static splash markup, a `<noscript>` hide rule and a fail-safe `setTimeout` in the existing theme-bootstrap IIFE; removed their `<div class="loading">`) and all five renderers (`landingPage`, `leaguePage`, `dashboardPage`, `playerPage`, `playerGeneralPage`); DELETED the `.logo-splash`/`.logo-splash-img`/`logo-breathe`/`.logo.logo-loading` block from `css/layout.css`. **Design:** logo centred in a ring of squares that fill clockwise from 12 o'clock, plus a stage label ("Fetching match history"), a `Step n of 6 · x%` counter and a six-chip checklist. The ring IS the progress bar — lit count is `round(progress × n)`. **Four things worth not re-deriving:** (1) **The markup is STATIC in the HTML, not built by the module.** ES modules are deferred, so anything JS creates paints after the first frame — which is exactly the "ugly static Loading… text" the redesign existed to kill. The module only fills the ring and removes it. Corollary: a module that fails to load would leave the splash covering the page forever, hence the 25s fail-safe `setTimeout` in each page's inline IIFE and the `<noscript>` rule. (2) **`startSplash()` self-invokes at the bottom of the module.** Renderers reach their own call only after their page's `await`s, and `await initNavBar()` on the landing page can block on the network for seconds — measured live, that left the static splash on screen with an empty ring. Import time is the earliest our JS runs. It no-ops when `#logo-splash` is absent, so importing is side-effect-free on pages without the markup. (3) **Progress creeps but never completes a stage on its own** — `creepTo()` eases toward `(idx + 0.92)/n` over 2.6s, so the ring keeps moving through a slow fetch without ever claiming a step finished; only a real `splashStage(key)` call closes the gap. Stages are fired from inside `Promise.all` via `.then(r => { splashStage('x'); return r; })` so each promise reports itself as it lands rather than the whole batch reporting at once. (4) **Theming has no per-theme block.** Everything derives from `--header-bg`/`--header-text`/`--color-accent` (the chrome pair is a designed dark-surface/light-ink couple in all 8 themes, which `--color-bg`/`--color-text` are not). Two derived knobs exist because two themes break the naive version: `--sp-lift` blends the accent toward the ink (Nature is green-on-green, X22 orange-on-near-black) and `--sp-alt` shades the second square colour toward the canvas (Vegas and Casino are gold-on-gold, so the alternation collapsed to one flat tone). **Geometry:** squares are flush by construction — side = `⌀ · sin(π/n)`, computed in CSS via `sin()`/`pi`, so it stays exact at every viewport with no resize handler; the logo takes the remaining inner diameter. The ring's box must be `⌀ + side` (squares are CENTRED on `⌀` and stick out half a side) or a low square count overflows into the chips below. | ◯ pending — v2 has `src/components/Splash/splash.{js,css}` marked `●` from Phase 3, but that is the OLD breathe-overlay design; it is now stale and must be rebuilt against this. | **v2:** rebuild `Splash` to this design and port `splashConfig.js` alongside it as a shared module (both the component and the editor tool import it — same relationship `HeroBanner`/`banner-config.json` has). **Do NOT make the component render its own markup into an empty page** — v2's Vite entry is still a deferred module, so the splash markup belongs in `index.html`'s shell (or an SSR'd shell) exactly as in v1, with the component hydrating it; otherwise the first paint regresses to blank. Keep the self-invoke-on-import (or a v2-native equivalent that runs before any router/data `await`), the 25s fail-safe and the `<noscript>` rule — all three exist because the markup outlives the JS. Keep the stage list in `splashConfig.js` mapped 1:1 to the real awaited promises; if v2's data path changes shape, update the stages with it or the splash narrates work the app no longer does. | 2026-08-02 | (staged) | **The splash flickered on every fast navigation — it appeared and vanished inside ~200ms, which reads as a glitch rather than feedback. Fixed with cross-document View Transitions + a show-delay, and two follow-on bugs the fix exposed.** Files: `css/layout.css` (NEW `@view-transition { navigation: auto }` + `::view-transition-old/new(root)` keyframes), `css/splash.css` (`:root.sp-defer .splash { opacity: 0 }` + an opacity transition), `js/utils/splash.js` (reveal-aware `shownAt`, immediate removal when never shown, don't rebuild an existing ring), and all five page HTMLs (defer logic in the inline IIFE, `modulepreload`, logo `preload`, inline ring builder, emptied the duplicate `Loading...` page title). **The UX rule:** a loader needs BOTH a delay before showing AND a minimum display once shown. We had only the minimum (450ms), which is precisely why fast loads flickered — the old pre-rewrite code had the delay (`SHOW_DELAY_MS`) and it was dropped. It is restored, but applied ONLY when `document.referrer` is same-origin: on an internal navigation the View Transition is still showing the previous page, so there is something to look at; on a direct hit or reload there is nothing behind the splash, so hiding it would just mean a blank screen. **`@view-transition` is the "previous screen as backdrop" the user asked for** — the browser snapshots the outgoing page, holds it while the next loads, then cross-fades (we blur the outgoing snapshot). It cannot be hand-rolled in an MPA: by the time the new document runs any JS, the old one is gone. Verified: `pagereveal` fires at 88ms, and on a fast nav the splash stays at `opacity 0.00` for its whole life and is removed at 164ms, before its 420ms reveal time — never seen. **Two bugs this exposed, both only visible under real throttling (CDP, 600ms RTT / 150KB/s):** (1) **The ring was missing exactly when the splash mattered.** It was built by `splash.js`, and an ES module only EXECUTES once its entire import graph has downloaded — `splash.js` is reached via the page's renderer, which pulls in dozens of files. Measured: still zero squares after 4 seconds. `modulepreload` does NOT fix this (it fetches, it does not execute). Affects all six deep-link destinations: `index.html?tab=records#records-{achievements,pr,match,league}`, `?tab=leaderboard#sections`, `?tab=players` (siteSidebar.js). **Root cause — THREE compounding bugs, found and fixed across three passes:** (1) `renderAchievementsSection`/`renderPRLeadersSection` fill their tables **asynchronously**, so scrolling on the very next frame left the target drifting once those sections grew — fixed by `await`ing `Promise.allSettled(types.map(buildAllTimeRankings))` before scrolling for `records-pr`/`records-match`/`records-league`. (2) A hardcoded CSS `scroll-margin-top: calc(var(--topbar-height) + ...)` compensated for the `position:fixed` top bar but is "blind to a fixed number" per user feedback — it'd silently go stale the moment anything changes at the top of the page (the in-flight hero-banner work, `css/hero-banner.css`/`banner-poc.html`, is the concrete near-term case) — replaced with a `measureFixedTopOffset()` helper that walks `document.body`'s/`#app`'s direct children, finds whatever is *currently rendered* `fixed`/`sticky` and pinned to the viewport top, and returns its live measured height + a 16px breathing-room constant; a manual `window.scrollTo()` uses this instead of `scrollIntoView`. (3) **The one that caused the intermittent/delayed symptom:** even after (1)+(2), Playwright-verified logging (`window.__sdbg` array probe, since MCP console capture proved unreliable mid-session) showed our own scroll landing correctly, holding for ~1.2s, then **the browser's own native "scroll to fragment" retry fired independently ~8-10s later** (once the page's slow resource loading — 13 pre-existing 404s from optional per-league JSON files — finally let the document settle) and smooth-animated the page to wherever the *browser* thinks the `#id` fragment belongs, with zero awareness of the fixed topbar, undoing our correct scroll. This affected `#sections` (Leaders/Players) reliably and would eventually hit the Records ids too on a slow-enough load. **Final fix (pass 3):** immediately strip the hash from the URL via `history.replaceState(history.state, '', location.pathname + location.search)` as soon as `hashId` is captured — with nothing left in `location.hash`, the browser never has a late native retry to fire; only our own `scrollTo` runs. (4) **Pass 4, user follow-up:** Players (`?tab=players`, no hash at all) and Leaders (`?tab=leaderboard#sections`) still showed the tab strip at the top even after (1)-(3) — because their scroll target was `shell.root` (`#sections`) itself, and the tab strip is `shell.root`'s own FIRST CHILD, so landing `shell.root`'s top at the viewport top necessarily shows the tab strip too (unlike Records, whose targets are the inner `records-*` divs, which sit AFTER the tab strip in the DOM so the strip scrolls out of view above them). **Fix:** gave the Leaderboard/Players panels their own stable ids — `shell.panels.leaderboard.id='leaderboard-panel'`, `shell.panels.players.id='players-panel'` — set right in `renderLandingPage` alongside the existing `shell.root.id='sections'`, and repointed the sidebar links (`js/render/siteSidebar.js`) at `#leaderboard-panel`/`#players-panel` instead of `#sections`/no-hash. Landing on the panel (a sibling AFTER the tab strip, not a container that starts WITH it) scrolls the tab strip out of view exactly like the Records case. Verified live (MCP, 390×844, 12s+ waits): all **six** destinations (`records-{achievements,pr,match,league}`, `leaderboard-panel`, `players-panel`) settle at a consistent ~79.5-80px with the tab strip's bottom edge (63.5px) fully hidden behind the topbar (64px), and stay there. | ◯ pending — v2 destination for the tab/scroll logic is `src/pages/landing/landing.js` (Phase 6, no v2 AppTabs/landing page built yet) | **v2:** when the landing page's `?tab=`/`#hash` deep-link scroll is built: (a) do NOT port "scroll on next frame" if any section above the target loads asynchronously — await those promises first; (b) do NOT hardcode a `scroll-margin-top`/offset constant for the fixed top bar — use a live DOM measurement (`measureFixedTopOffset()`'s approach, or a v2-native equivalent reading the actual `TopBar` component's rendered height via a ref) so it's immune to future top-of-page chrome changes; (c) **strip `location.hash` via `history.replaceState` as soon as it's read, before doing your own scroll** — the browser's native fragment-scroll retry can fire many seconds after initial load (tied to when the document "settles," which a slow/erroring resource load can push out significantly) and will silently override a correct custom scroll with zero regard for fixed chrome; (d) **every deep-link target must be a node that comes AFTER the tab strip in the DOM, never the tab-shell container itself** — give each tab's `TabPanel` (or its default first section) a stable id and link straight to it, so the tab strip is always scrolled out of view above every destination, uniformly. Any framework router that owns hash-based scroll restoration (e.g. if v2 adopts one) must confirm it disables its own native-anchor behavior the same way. Verify this class of bug with a *sustained* poll (several seconds, not just an immediate check) — an immediate-only check can pass while the delayed native override still lurks. This is the general form of the "fixed top bar needs reserved spacing" bug — see the Admin page-title row below for the sibling case in `.admin-main`, which still uses a static CSS constant since it isn't scroll-driven (a fixed `padding-top` is appropriate there; only scroll-target math needs to be dynamic). |

| 2026-06-30 | (staged) | **iOS search-sheet — sidesteps Safari's focus-zoom on sub-16px inputs (per-platform: iOS overlay, Android inline-enlarge).** NEW `js/render/searchOverlay.js` + `css/search-overlay.css`. On **iOS only** (detected once → `html.is-ios`; `?searchoverlay=force`/`=off` override for desktop testing), a capture-phase `mousedown` `preventDefault` blocks the inline `.app-search-input` from focusing (so iOS never zooms — classic keep-focus trick; mousedown not pointer/touch so the click survives), and a capture-phase `click` (`stopPropagation`, so the field's own focus/click handlers never fire) opens a `position:fixed` sheet pinned to `visualViewport.offsetTop` whose input is **`font-size:16px`** (at threshold → no zoom). Smart-search results render below; picking either navigates (sidebar) or writes the value back into the source field + dispatches `input` so its existing handler runs in place. The page never zooms and never scrolls, so closing restores the exact position with the field filled. Per-field behaviour via a `registerSearchAdapter(input,{suggest,pick})` registry (WeakMap); `<input list=...>` datalist fields (rem-b6c, admin match/round filters) use an automatic default adapter (read `<option>`s, write back) — **zero per-field code**; sidebar/matchup/What-If register small adapters at their mount sites. `searchEntities(query)` was **extracted** from `navigation.js`'s `mountSearchInto` so the flyout AND the sheet share one matcher. Install is idempotent + iOS-gated, called from `mountSiteSidebar` (public) and `adminPage.js` (admin). **Two follow-up fixes (same day, real-iOS testing):** (1) the sheet uses ONE delegated **`pointerup`** handler (with a `click` fallback for desktop), NOT `click`/`mousedown`. Root cause: when the sheet input is focused (keyboard up), iOS treats the first tap elsewhere as a keyboard-dismiss and **swallows the synthesized `click` AND `mousedown`** on that tap — so neither fires on the option/`✕`/scrim (an earlier `mousedown` attempt did NOT fix it). Raw Pointer Events aren't synthesized-mouse events, so `pointerup` fires normally and also respects scroll (a drag emits `pointercancel`, not `pointerup`). The handler routes `[data-close]` (the `✕` button AND the full-viewport backdrop scrim) → cancel, and `.search-sheet-option` → select; Enter re-uses the same `selectItem`. So a tap anywhere on the dimmed backdrop cancels, like `✕`. (2) Result rows now show the **league glyph / player avatar+status-dot** like the flyout: items carry an optional pre-escaped `iconHtml`, and the flyout's `.search-icon*` rules in `css/navigation.css` were broadened to `:is(.nav-search-results, .search-sheet-results) …` so BOTH surfaces share one icon definition (no duplication). **Android path** (other half of the same decision): `css/layout.css` gains `@media (pointer:coarse){ html:not(.is-ios) .app-search-input{ font-size:16px } }` — Android coarse-pointer phones just enlarge the inline field to 16px (no overlay; Android has no focus-zoom bug); iOS excluded (uses the sheet); desktop (fine pointer) keeps `--fs-090`. CSS `<link>` added to all 6 pages. **Revised 2026-07-03 — all-mobile + reliable close (SUPERSEDES the per-platform split above):** per user decision the sheet now runs on **every touch device**, not iOS-only. Gating is `isTouchDevice()` (`matchMedia('(pointer:coarse)')` OR `navigator.maxTouchPoints>0` OR `'ontouchstart' in window`, plus the `?searchoverlay=force`/`=off` override). Consequently the `html.is-ios` class AND the Android `@media(pointer:coarse){ html:not(.is-ios) .app-search-input{font-size:16px} }` inline-enlarge rule were **DELETED** — all mobile uses the sheet; inline fields stay `--fs-090` and are never focused (a tap opens the sheet). Close reliability rewritten again: **`✕` + backdrop scrim now fire on `pointerdown`** (the earliest touch event — fires on finger-down before the OS interprets a keyboard-dismiss, so it can't be swallowed, and before scroll) while **option-select stays on `pointerup`** (scroll-safe); `closeOverlay()` stamps `lastCloseTs` and the global open-handler ignores opens for 500ms after a close, killing the "ghost-click reopen" where the synthesized click after closing lands on the field underneath. The typo-editor forces the flag for ANY non-desktop device (`isTouchDevice(viewport)` = device group ≠ 'desktop'), not just Apple. | ◯ pending — v2 destination is the `SearchBox`/`FormField` primitives (Phase 2/3). | **v2:** implement the same per-platform anti-zoom **once** in the primitives, not per consumer. `SearchBox`/`FormField` should (a) on iOS open a 16px sheet (port `searchOverlay.js` as a `SearchSheet` driven by the same `suggest`/`pick` adapter shape; reuse the v2 equivalent of `searchEntities`), and (b) on Android coarse-pointer enlarge the inline control to 16px via `html:not(.is-ios)`. Datalist-style fields get the default read-`<option>`s/write-back adapter for free. Keep `is-ios` as the single platform flag set at boot. **`SearchSheet` must drive option-select + close via a delegated **`pointerup`** (never `click`/`mousedown`)** — with the input focused, iOS swallows the synthesized click/mousedown of the keyboard-dismiss first-tap; pointerup fires natively and respects scroll. Route `[data-close]` (✕ + full-viewport backdrop scrim) → cancel and `.search-sheet-option` → select in one handler, so a backdrop tap cancels like ✕. Its result rows should reuse the shared `SearchResultRow`/`.search-icon*` icon styling (one definition for flyout + sheet, mirroring the `:is()` broadening). Pairs with the canonical-`.app-search-input` row below (one field type → one anti-zoom strategy). |

| 2026-06-24 | (staged) | **F3 Round Editor — restore the whole-row state tint after the FF inset adoption + stable pending hover.** File: `css/admin.css`. **Root cause:** when FF adopted the `--color-inset` body well (2026-06-24 row below), it added `.admin-table.font-large tbody td { background: var(--color-inset) }` (spec 0,2,2). The F3 match-state rules were `.match-block-overridden td` etc. (spec **0,1,1**) → out-specified, so the FF inset masked the override/pending tint on **every** data cell. The first-col "reconciliation" rules meant to defend the sticky column were written as `tbody .match-block-X td:first-child` — a **descendant** selector, but the match-block **is** the `<tbody>` itself (`<tbody class="match-block match-block-overridden">`), so they **never matched** either. Net effect (verified live via MCP, default+dark+vegas): a saved-override row rendered fully `--color-inset` — the "different colour after Save" was completely gone, not just partial. **Fix (A):** the three state rules now read `.admin-table.font-large.admin-round-table tbody.match-block-{overridden,pending,unplayed} td` (compound `tbody.match-block-X`, spec **0,4,2**) → beats the FF inset base and tints the **whole** row incl. both sticky first columns (overridden→`accent-light`, pending→`warning-bg`, unplayed→`inset`+`opacity:.6`); the now-redundant + broken first-col resting reconciliation block was deleted. **Fix (B) — hover unified to whole-block, per state:** the old F3 hover was per-cell/per-sub-row (granular `.match-row-a:hover td:not(...)` etc.) **plus** a fragile sticky-first-col reconciliation block — and the pending state had no hover rule at all, so before Save a `.match-block-pending` row flipped some cells to plain `--color-hover` (the general + FF sticky-col hover out-specified the 0,4,2 resting tint). Replaced the **entire** hover system (granular rules + first-col reconciliation, deleted) with **one rule per state**: `.admin-table.font-large.admin-round-table tbody.match-block-X:has(:hover) td` (spec **0,5,2** — beats FF's inset body, row hover (0,3,2) and sticky-col hover (0,4,3), so the whole row incl. the sticky first col flips together and **no first-col reconciliation is needed**). **Per-block colour model (user-specified):** each match-block is always ONE uniform colour — 4 resting states [played→`--color-inset`, unplayed→`--color-inset`+`opacity:.6`, pending→`--color-warning-bg`, overridden→`--color-accent-light`] **+ (5) hover→`--color-hover`, STATUS-INDEPENDENT**. Hover is a **single** rule `tbody.match-block:has(:hover) td { background: var(--color-hover); opacity: 1 }` (spec 0,5,2 — beats FF's inset/row-hover/sticky-col-hover **and** the per-state resting tints at 0,4,2) that flips the whole block to one colour for **every** status (played/unplayed/pending/overridden alike); `opacity:1` overrides unplayed's resting `.6` so it hovers at full strength. **No per-state hover overrides** (the earlier overridden-mix / pending-stable hover variants were removed at the user's request for a single status-independent hover colour). Verified live (MCP): overridden/played/unplayed resting = uniform per block; hovering any sub-row (incl. row-b) flips all cells of the block to `--color-hover` @ opacity 1. Docs: `docs/TABLE-DESIGN.md` FF param table ("F3 match-block state tint" row) + `docs/TABLE-DESIGN-full.md` match-block states table. | ◯ pending — Phase 8 (`src/pages/admin/roundEditor.css`) | **v2:** when `roundEditor.css` is built, the match-block state tint MUST out-specify the `FormTable` inset body well **and** target the tbody's own state class (`tbody.match-block-X td`, not a descendant — the match-block is the tbody). Tint the whole row incl. the sticky first col. **Hover model = one whole-block `:has(:hover)` rule, status-independent** — every match-block (played/unplayed/pending/overridden) flips to a single `--color-hover` (colour E) with `opacity:1`; no per-state hover overrides, no separate first-col hover reconciliation. The 4 resting state tints are uniform per block. This is the "F3↔FF reconciliation (match-block state colors)" noted in the `css/admin.css` file-map row above. |

| 2026-06-05 | (staged) | **Championship predictor — simulation model overhaul.** Files: `js/compute/championshipPredictor.js`, `js/compute/crossLeague.js`, `js/render/dashboardPage.js` (predictor + what-if call-sites, info popup, Last-300 source). Behaviour changes: **(0)** New `batchLast300PRForSimulator(playerNames)` in `crossLeague.js` pools **all non-REGULAR leagues** (doubling + ubc) into one Last-300 window (weight 7), returning `{mean, std}`; the dashboard predictor now uses this regardless of the league's own type (shared core `computeLast300Map` extracted; the old per-type `batchLast300PR` stays for site-wide display). **(1)** Always Monte Carlo — exact enumeration / Gray-code path removed; no more ≤20/>20 branching. Each remaining match, **every iteration**, draws both players' PR fresh from N(Last-300 mean, std) (Marsaglia-polar `gaussian()`), then looks up the win prob via `getWinProbability` — now **linearly interpolated** between adjacent PR-diff rows (was `Math.round`), benefiting `luckPercentile.js` too. **(2)** REGULAR uses the same combined Last-300 PR. **(3)** REGULAR Win-Rate ties resolved by a progressively-narrowing cascade over the still-tied subgroup: (a) head-to-head wins, (b) internal points-diff, (c) total points-diff vs league, (d) alphabetical — implemented via `rankRegular`/`resolveRegularTie` + base pairwise tables built from played-match scores passed in as new `playedMatches` param; a synthesized match scores winner = MatchLength, loser = ⌈ML/2⌉ (`winMargin`). UBC PR point now goes to the lower **drawn** PR (removed `prWinProbability`/normal-CDF PR-point path). Removed dead `simulateExact`/`findChampion`. **PR-based leagues (Doubling/UBC) secondary tiebreak is now a per-run Mean PR**: each iteration accumulates the same drawn PRs into `prSum` (seeded from `playedPRSum = leagueMeanPR·gamesPlayed`) and divides by `finalGames` — replacing the old static `tiebreakerPR` blend (which now serves only the X=0 season-complete path, where it collapses to the real recorded Mean PR). **Sampler:** production default is an inverse-CDF LUT (`gaussianLUT`, 4096-cell probit table via Acklam, ~32KB, lazy-built, RAM-only) + interpolated table lookup — chosen after a 12-run/1M-sim A/B on real June data showed identical rankings (maxΔ ≤ 0.06 pts, below MC noise) at ~30% less wall-time vs polar. `simOpts = { iterationsOverride, useLUT, useInterp }` keeps polar + round-lookup reachable for A/B; defaults `useLUT:true, useInterp:true`. **Iteration floor:** `estimateIterations` STEP lowered 200k→50k (both the rounding granularity and the floor) — large leagues (where the 500ms budget buys < STEP) pin to 50k. Measured on JUNE (25 players, ~220 remaining): ~0.8s @ MoE ±0.42% (was ~3.6s @ ±0.21% at the 200k floor); rankings stayed stable across 50k/100k/200k. Small leagues stay time-bound above the floor (unaffected). | ◯ pending in v2 (predictor phase) | Port `championshipPredictor.js` and the `crossLeague` Last-300 helpers verbatim. Key invariants for v2: predictor PR source must pool all non-regular leagues (not the dashboard league's type); engine is Monte-Carlo-only with per-match per-iteration PR draws feeding the interpolated table; REGULAR needs the a→b→c→alphabetical cascade and must receive played matches (with scores) to seed the pairwise tables. Numeric regression check available: the 4-player tie scenario in this session resolves P1>P2>P3>P4 deterministically. |

Tie groups are now keyed on **WinRate only** (previously required equal WinRate *and* wins, and only did criterion (a) + alphabetical). Fix: the effective transition block (bottom of file) now closes with `var(--flyout-close-delay, 0.25s)` on opacity+transform, and the open-state rule (`:hover`/`.pinned`/`:focus-within`) sets `transition-delay: 0s` so opening stays instant. The μ/σ popup emits one group **per charted player, in row order** (name heading + the series' μ/σ line + one explainer block per series), so it reads as a walk down the stack; the name heading is suppressed when there is only one chart, where it would just repeat the sentence beneath it. NEW `drawMultiHistogramRow()` in `prCorrelationChart.js` — `drawHistogramRow` was left untouched. **Mark geometry (load-bearing, settled after three attempts):** ONE series draws as **bars**; **two or more switch to continuous frequency-polygon LINES**, one per series in the series' colour, through the bin centres and anchored to 0% half a bin outside each end. Both bar arrangements were built and rejected on the way here — side-by-side sub-bars (a split bin reads as two different PR gaps) and nested/inset overlapping bars (reads as one stacked total). Lines share the exact same X slots and never occlude one another. **The fit is a fourth state, not an overlay in every mode:** with ONE series it is drawn over the bars in the shared warning orange (model vs data); with SEVERAL it **replaces** the data lines and each curve keeps its series' colour — drawing both would be six near-parallel strokes in three colours. `rowHeight` (the player page passes **152**, double the shared 76) and a gridline step that follows the range (1/2/5%) are what make a fitted normal read as a bell instead of a flat arc; the fit-only view also drives the Y scale off the curve peaks rather than the bars. The "All" series gets a third hue — NEW `--color-series-all` (violet, deliberately constant across themes, same reasoning as `--color-warning`) — so it collides with neither win-green, loss-red, nor the accent blue already used by links/buttons/the density ramp. **(3)** `makePgSection` gained `headerHtml`, injected before `wireSectionCollapse` runs so a heading "?" is picked up by sectionCollapse's info-button rule (click opens, never collapses). These are the first `page: 'player'` popups, so `explanation-and-maths.html` gained a `player` entry in `PAGE_LABEL` plus a mirror of the `.pg-gauss-block` separator rule (the tool loads no project CSS). and `player.html` now loads `css/dashboard.css` for the shared `.predictor-tooltip` / `.predictor-info-popup` / `.corr-*` chrome (that file is entirely class/id-scoped, so it adds nothing else). | 2026-08-06 | (working tree) | **Fix: a REGULAR league never appeared in its year's Annual Leaderboard.** Files: `js/utils/helpers.js`, `js/render/landingPage.js`, `js/presets/playerLeaguesPreset.js`. Root cause is the league-id date parser, not the leaderboard: `parseLeagueDate` assumed the id ENDS with `<Month> <Year>` (it read `parts[len-1]`/`parts[len-2]`). Since two leagues of different types can run in the same month, their ids can't collide, so a regular league is stored as **`"July 2026 Regular"`** — under that assumption it parsed to `year: null, monthIndex: -1`, and `buildAllLeaderboards` dropped it via `if (isNaN(year) \|\| monthIndex < 0) continue`. (The same `continue` guard was also broken in its own right: `parseLeagueDate` returns **null**, not NaN, so `isNaN(null) === false` — only the `monthIndex` half was actually rejecting anything.) The league still showed in **Completed Leagues** because that table prefers `params.IssueDate` over the id — the two surfaces disagreeing is exactly why a league could read as completed yet be missing from its year. **Fix:** `parseLeagueDate` now SCANS all tokens for a month name (case-insensitive) and a 4-digit year anywhere in the id, so a type suffix (or any other trailing token) no longer blinds it; `monthShort` falls back to `null` instead of an arbitrary token. `buildAllLeaderboards` guards on `year == null` and resolves the date via a new `leagueYearMonth(l)` helper. **Superseded in part by the row below** — the interim fix took the month from the ID first and `IssueDate` second (because the cloud's `"July 2026"` carried `issue_date 2026-06-01`, so trusting the date labelled that column "Jun" and produced **two colliding "Jun" columns**, caught live via MCP). That was treating the symptom: the date itself was wrong data. The leaderboard now reads `IssueDate` and nothing else — see the next row. The parser fix also silently repairs three other id-derived paths that were filtering regular leagues out (`playerPage.js` league timeline, `playerGeneralPage.js` latest-league flag source, `navigation.js` year field); `formatLeagueDate` now guards the null `monthShort`. **Verified (MCP, cloud data):** 2026 gains a **Regular** pill alongside Doubling, with Jul + Aug columns, 25 rows and no PR column (correct — REGULAR has no PR); the Doubling panel is unchanged at Jan–Aug with no duplicate month. | ◯ pending — v2 landing/leaderboards not yet built | **v2:** `v2/src/utils/formatting.js` `parseLeagueDate` is a **verbatim copy of the broken v1 version** (trailing-two-token assumption) — port the token-scanning implementation. Never assume a league id ends with its date; ids carry type suffixes. Return `null` (not NaN) and have callers guard on `== null`. For the annual leaderboard specifically, derive the month from the ID first and only fall back to `IssueDate` — real data has leagues whose IssueDate disagrees with their name, and a month column keyed off the wrong month collides with another league's column. |

Do NOT show an exit node reconstructed from the clicks timeline (it is pageview-derived and absent for read-and-leave / zero-click visits); if an exit is ever wanted, take it from the server's `exit_page`. The SQL session aggregate must carry `entry_league_id` (first pageview's league) alongside `entry_page`/`entry_player`. |

| 2026-09-04 | (working tree) | **The "why is this report unassigned" chain reports what it MEASURED — `sql/mail_reason_accuracy.sql`.** New file: `sql/mail_reason_accuracy.sql` (run AFTER `mail_sync.sql` and `mail_rescan_pending.sql` — it redefines `mail_reports_pending()` once more). (A branch for "an open fixture that should have matched but did not" was drafted and then dropped: the two rules agree today, so it described no reachable state, and a branch guarding against a hypothetical future divergence is itself a guess about causes.) **`Not scheduled to meet`**: a claim about what the schedule intended, when all the step knows is that the running leagues hold no fixture for the pair — whether they were *meant* to meet depends on the league's format, which nothing in the DB records. That sentence is what made September 2026's incomplete schedule read as a deliberate one for a month. Now `Not scheduled to play — same league, no fixture between them`. The first half is a fact, not a guess: the fixture list IS the schedule and holds no row for this pair. The second half is what makes the first safe — it states that a suitable running league DOES hold both players, so an empty fixture reads as something to look at rather than a closed answer. What the format ought to contain is still not claimed, because the schema does not record it. Signature change: `mail_orphan_reason` takes the two scores, so the impossible-score step lives inside the chain instead of in the caller (a chain split across two files is a chain whose order is invisible), and it moved BELOW the name steps at the owner's call — a report wrong in both ways is more usefully described by the name than the number. `mail_resolve_report` keeps testing the score FIRST: there it is not a message but the gate between a score of 1004 and an automatic apply. `mail_reports_pending` also forces `candidates` empty on an impossible score, since live candidates would otherwise hand such a report a league picker and an Apply button that `mail_apply_report` can only refuse. Renders as a muted dashed chip (`.clog-nav`), deliberately NOT styled like a UI click (it is chrome the browser performed, not a control the user pressed). Scope: nav rows join the transitions **log** (per-occurrence, with exact time) but NOT the aggregated "Top navigation flows" summary, so refresh self-loops don't distort a popularity ranking. Verified end-to-end on the local DB: a real browser Back (league→landing) emitted `nav_type=back` with the correct source league, a Refresh emitted both a pageview and a `nav_type=reload` self-transition at the same instant, and `analytics_summary` surfaced both in `transitions_log`/`clicks_log`/session timeline with `click_count` including the navs (rolled back after). Cloud deploy order matters: run `sql/analytics_poc.sql` (adds the `nav_type` column) BEFORE shipping the JS, or PostgREST drops every insert on the unknown column — the same lesson as `moved_banner`/`hub`. | ◯ pending — v2 not yet built | **v2:** carry `nav_type` as a click sub-kind, not a new `event_type`, so the three surfaces (counts / clicks log / transitions) include it for free and only the transitions filter needs widening. | 2026-09-04 | (working tree) | **A player becomes an entity — `sql/players_registry.sql`.** New file: `sql/players_registry.sql` (run after `site_bundle.sql`; `mail_reason_accuracy.sql` depends on it). A league is a row in `public.leagues` and can exist before its first fixture; a PLAYER had no such row — a person exists only by appearing in `matches.player_a/player_b`, and `players_metadata` is decoration on an already-existing player (5 rows against a 60-name roster), not the roster. With no entity there was no authoritative answer to "does this name exist", so each caller invented one and they disagreed: the public Players tab counted ANY league, `mail_orphan_reason` counted only RUNNING ones. Consequence: a player of five seasons who was between leagues was reported to the admin as `Unknown player: fridlich`, and with every league stopped between seasons EVERY name read as unknown. `public.players_registry` is that definition written once — a VIEW, deliberately not a table, so it derives from the fixtures and cannot drift the way a table would need triggers to avoid. It exposes `leagues_count` / `visible_leagues_count` / `in_running_league(_any)` so each caller states its OWN visibility rule without re-deriving the roster. Cost: none — `get_site_bundle` already ships every match and all of players_metadata in one call, so the client always held the raw material; `players` is one more aggregate in a query already running, no extra round trip, and the derivation moves off every client. The chain now splits two facts that used to share one message: `Unknown player: X` (in no league, ever) and `Not in a running league: X` / `Neither is in a running league` (known, nowhere to play right now) — the latter also replacing `No running league`, since naming the people beats a message about the system. The view also carries `last_flag` / `last_league` — the CONTEXT-FREE flag, resolved through the newest league in DisplayOrder that is not hidden or archived, i.e. the identical rule to `js/utils/playerFlags.js`'s `latest()`. Identity facts belong here; STATISTICS deliberately do not: mean PR depends on `manual_overrides` and league-type rules that live in `compute/stats.js`, so a SQL copy would recreate exactly the two-definitions drift this file exists to end — and the bundle already ships every match, so precomputing saves no bytes, only client CPU (which rule 10 already answers by gating compute on visibility). Building `last_flag` surfaced the decision that matters: a league ABSENT from DisplayOrder must be excluded outright, not merely sorted last. Every public page walks DisplayOrder (`crossLeague.js`'s `loadAllLeagues` builds its whole array from it), so such a league is invisible site-wide, and a left join resolving a flag through one disagreed with `playerFlags.js` on exactly the one player whose only league DisplayOrder had lost. Verified on the running site against the local DB: the Players tab renders 60 rows, identical names/statuses/order before and after, `loadPlayersRegistry()` confirmed returning 60 (so the new path is the one that ran), and `last_flag` matches BOTH `playerFlags.js`'s `latest()` and the flag A7 actually renders, for all 60 players, 0 disagreements. League and player pages re-checked for runtime errors (none); all four guard scripts pass. | ◯ pending — v2 not yet built | **v2:** give a player a real row from day one. A view is the cheap fix for a schema that already shipped; a schema being designed should not need it. |

Note the model's own limit, unchanged here: `match_history` is `unique (league_id, player_a, player_b)` — one row per pairing holding the CURRENT value plus when it last changed. It cannot express two versions of a result, so a corrected match does not appear with its OLD score in an older snapshot; it simply vanishes from it. | 2026-09-04 | (working tree) | **An update point is a MATCH, and the picker shows which — plus the revert that made the count true.** Files: `js/compute/matchHistory.js` (`getUpdatePoints` rebuilt; new `resultSides`/`describeResult`/`formatUpdateDay`; `getMatchesAsOf` takes a point value), `js/data/matchHistoryReconcile.js` (the revert rule), `js/render/dashboardPage.js` (new `mountSnapshotPicker`, both `<select>`s replaced), `js/utils/combobox.js` + `js/render/searchOverlay.js` (new `nameHtml` decoration), `css/dashboard.css`, new `scripts/check-match-history-reconcile.mjs`. The owner's rule: **the number of update points equals the number of rows in Played Matches (B5), plus Initial** — every match the league counts is a place you can rewind to, and nothing else is. Three things stood in the way. **(1) Points were grouped by MINUTE**, so a league imported in one go offered 3 points for 89 matches (measured: 89 rows carrying 3 distinct timestamps, identical to the millisecond — the data has no per-match time to recover). Grouping is gone; a point is a match. Since a timestamp no longer identifies one, `value` gained an optional `#n` ordinal — "the state after the nth match of that instant", in the arbitrary-but-stable order of `orderTimeline()` — and a bare timestamp still means the whole instant, so `?asof=` links shared before this keep working. Matches sharing an instant are labelled by their DAY, not by a clock that cannot tell them apart. **(2) The pickers were `<select>`s**, and a native option holds text and nothing else, so a row could not say who beat whom. Both now mount the canonical `mountSearchField` through one shared `mountSnapshotPicker()`, rendering "date · winner **beats** loser" with each side as the project's identity chip (`playerIdentityHtml`: flag · name · title badges, em-relative per the V2 principles). The picker filters as a side effect — typing a name narrows a 91-row list to that player's 8 matches — and the ‹ › steppers keep their old meaning. **(3) Cancelling an override did nothing.** `computeMatchHistoryReconcile` protected any `source: 'manual'` row from the CSV pass unconditionally, so a deleted override left its values in `match_history` forever — and because history OUTRANKS the source in the read-side merge, the cancelled result kept showing on the public site while the admin watched the override vanish from the editor. A manual row is now protected only while its override is LIVE; otherwise the pairing reverts to what the source says (= the DB before the manual change), dated `now` when the values actually move and keeping its stored date when they do not. A technical result on a match nobody played, once cancelled, has no played row to revert to and leaves history entirely — so it contributes no point, exactly as the rule requires. Seven invariants of that module are now an executable guard (`check-match-history-reconcile.mjs`), because every rule in it was written after a corruption bug and none of them fails loudly. The whole `#n` mechanism exists because a bulk import stamps many matches with one instant, and the timestamp was doing double duty as both "when" and "which". Two rules to carry over regardless: a picker whose subject is not a single plain name cannot be a `<select>`, so plan the rich-row hook into the shared field from the start rather than bolting it on; and **an undo must travel the same path as the edit** — the reconcile knew how to apply an override and had no idea how to unapply one, which is the shape of bug that looks like nothing at all in review. |
